| **Kellan Renn** | Has the Halden yard, Tuesdays and Thursdays, nothing owed. On Seven's book "as a blade who's told where he's wanted." His mother signs "A. Renn, grader." |
| **Jude Talley** | Kill by name; 7.8 drams by name. Will stand at Saltmere, asked by name. Wants to see the sea. |
| **Pru Finch** | Her own sheet, her own red tag, the cord. Nipped to the first joint once. Goes to the night van with Kiva. |
| **Noa Bexley** | Reader to the Chair's office. His page reads *Not yet weighed* and *Not one thing.* Three columns before the Charter Board, Mr. Adlam second. |
| **Selka Dray** | Left leg broken above the knee and burned with cold on the 17th of the ninth month; in a chair; "on my feet by the third month." Unpersuaded, on the record twice. Means to beat Kiva in a lane at Saltmere, on a declared count, with a needle on her. Not a friend. |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Graywater. Wrote to the board; the brackets came off. Burned a card that said *mine.* |
| **Pike** | Graywater's catcher, six-and-twenty years. Will have Kiva as his second. |
| **Nairn** | Graywater's second blade; the leg healed; walking without the stick. |
| **Hollis Vane** | Home Line foreman ("the Sag"). Three directions to Kiva in his ledger, two with a cost column. |
| **Ysra Crest** | Placement Chair. Posted "seated." Keeps the *Witnessed* page. |
| **Healer Aske** | Keeps Kiva's card. Would not round the price down. |
| **Lowen Brask** | Old Four; named the hold and the paid-out string; two to the bar. |
| **Examiner Rusk, Mr. Adlam, Lumb, Ansty, A. Renn, Officer Yeo** | As in the Movement Six checkpoint. Yeo went south on the 8th and will ask for the Works' reader again in the spring. |

## 6. The grid and the rifts

- **The Ferren bank** (twenty-four cells, the Municipal Works') **is out of step.** The method:
  nothing in the water that keeps time; pumps stopped; every cell quenched on one word; fresh
  cans, each salted with a pinch of plain ash that "has never heard a beat"; a card with its
  own count under each handle, "not two alike in this room"; a reader between each cell and
  the next; each pump restarted on its own eight cells. It needs the main to be **still.**
- **Seven drums remain in step** on the Brickfields trunk: **19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 27, 28,** each
  alone on an open link, to be done one a day with the wardens' old winter stock. None has been
  done on the page.
- **Drum twenty-nine is fouled** (about thirty drams through a split plank). Its four streets
  are on boxes for about a month from the 6th of the tenth month.
- The Stopgate sluice works. The Crooked Line School's core house never fell in.
- **Saltmere's harbour bank, thirty-six cells, went into step at twenty past two on the 17th of
  the ninth month and has not come out.** Its pumps have not dropped a stroke. Nobody at
  Saltmere knows how to take a bank out.
- **Nobody has said** that water can step a cell, why a tear throws stepped ash, why husks
  stood a hold, or why the bank fell in on the 17th.
- The Home Line's suspension of held tears has not been lifted on the page.
- A can of civic stock in the Works' own store was cut with soot. Noticed; nobody has taken
  it up.
- **Evidence.** The notch on Kiva's pen falls eleven seconds after her second cliff (three
  occasions). The lamps' T-0 falls when a hold comes down. They are two things. T-0 entries
  on record: 2446, 2604, 2676, and three in 2680 (the Pit twice, Stopgate once). Noa's three
  columns (people; rifts and spawn; Saltmere) all rise, faster since the fifth month. One
  unvouched line says an overseas port's bank fell in at the same hour.

Renn, Lumb, and Mr. Adlam were out of earshot and **do not know that Kiva has dreamed** |

## 8. Institutions

- The Regional result stands without brackets: **1. Halden License. 2. Old Four. 3. Team
  Twenty-Eight. 4. T7.** "The first company goes forward to the National Measure, at Saltmere,
  in the third month, 2681. Entries under each team's own hand."
- Circuit rules made in 2680 and still in force: a gate shall be left lit; any catcher may
  red-tag a bag on stated grounds; no ash above I-trace on any Circuit floor; every Measure
  dram combed at the gate.
- The Charter Board has three sheets under the Chair's seal. It has not answered.
- The Order of the Even Hand's offer of a year as a guest, one door, is "on the latch."
- The Crooked Line School's tenth edition restores the lane in seven columns, with Kiva's
  lines in the fourth and sixth.

## 9. Unresolved threads carried in

The cause of the Step. Why a hold stood. What the notch and the lamps are. Who else has
dreamed. The filter. The disk's purpose and first book. Kiva's classification. Whether three
doors can be made safe, taught, or done a fourth time. Saltmere's bank. The seven drums. The
red bags and the court claim. The cut can. The Charter Board. Hart. Dray. Rhea's "we look
again." Kellan's four. The warrant. The reserve that cannot be made up. Mara's sealed page.
The overseas line.

## 11. Reconciled for Book Two

1. **Hart's hand.** Orla says in chapter 8 that he "lost a hand" and left "with one hand."
   His card says "the hand, the bad one." Both stand: Orla never saw him afterward. He has one
   working hand and one ruined one that is still on his arm.
2. **Who dreams.** The series bible says that "a small number of high-rank practitioners"
   record the fragments. Book One's second account is a pump keeper's. The accepted book
   governs: the fever reaches ordinary people who sit beside banks and lamps; high-rank
   accounts are fuller. Book Two's plan builds on both.
3. **The notch.** The Movement Three block's "about forty seconds" was the keeper's guess and
   is superseded: eleven seconds.
4. **Kellan's "five heads."** Left open by Book One and settled inside Book Two (see
   `BOOK_MAP.md`).
5. **The seven drums** are 19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 27, and 28. Twenty-nine is fouled, not stepped.

## Movement One block — chapters 1–8 (first draft, 2026-10-13)

Status: **from first-draft prose, unreviewed.** Everything here is on the page of
`manuscript/chapter-01.md` to `chapter-08.md`. The full record is
`provenance/MOVEMENT-001-CONTINUITY.md`. If review or repair changes the prose, this block is
corrected from the surviving text. Span: Monday the 17th of the tenth month, 2680, to Saturday
the 30th of the first month, 2681.

### M1.1 Kiva Rowan

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified.** On the Home Line's roll; not yet called.
- Dark for eighty-one days. **Each door relit singly under Aske's eye on Friday the 1st of
  the first month, 2681.**
- **Aske's card:** single doors, declared, one at a time; a crossing, one in seven days *at
  the most,* hand seen the next morning; the three-door "not on my card."
- **Re-read on the Circuit's instruments, Monday the 4th:** Draw **1.6** (was 3.1). Hold:
  Edge **3.1** (3.0), Ward **4.5** (2.6), Stance **6.5** (6.2). Rank, one door declared and
  two held shut: **Flame.** First door: none. With two doors used in sequence (one certified
  crossing, Thursday the 7th, read by Noa): **dashes;** "none shown." Not a classification.
- **Crossings:** one in this movement, Ember to still, 0.30 (0.26 the gap), off her own
  thimble. Hand to Ember: none; not certified. **Three doors: three clean; not asked; not
  done.**
- **Body:** first two left fingers as before; arm as it was; knee strapped.
- **The two fingers, proved (4th of the twelfth month):** through something solid, at a light
  touch, they say that a thing keeps time (six in seven); never what time; they read her own
  pulse if she leans or is frightened. "A reason to look." Not healing.
- **The disk:** cold. One fourth-column line (the 8th of the first month).
- **The dream:** no recurrence. Known in full to Mara alone, still.
- **The Chair's conditions for 2681:** as for 2680, re-entered line by line; at Saltmere her
  door is declared "by her captain's voice"; **one incident at a sanctioned board ends her
  season.**
- **Writing:** a copy of her own method sent to J. Hart (six sheets; three squares ruled off;
  one line left out and declared); **ten sheets of Pike's craft in seven columns;** a drill
  sheet begun.

### M1.2 Team Seven

- **Entry for the National Measure signed and the warrant paid, Friday the 29th of the first
  month.** *Captain: Cade, P., carry. Catcher: Rowan, K. Hold: Pryce, A. Kill: Talley, J.
  Reserve: Finch, P., catcher.*
- **Kellan Renn stands for Halden License** (*Blade: Renn, K.;* captain Pettit). His name
  stays on Seven's book for calls in Meridian.
- **Jude Talley is on the team book from the entry.**
- **The book:**

| Line | Drams |
|---|---:|
| Carried in | 25.53 |
| Floor, the 22nd of the tenth | −0.32 |
| Floor, the 16th of the first | −0.57 |
| Warrant, five heads at 3.0 | −15.00 |
| **Held** | **9.64** |
| Spoken for: the Marshal's floor (4.0); hand to Ember, six goes (1.8) | 5.80 |
| **Uncommitted** | **3.84** |
| **Sealed medical (W, plain)** | **0.8, unopened** |

- No plain ward-trace is to be had in Meridian or from Saltmere's shelf.
- **License:** second step in its own right; **no Flask license in its own right.** By the
  Board's notice a four without one hunts attached.
- Seven lost a yard contest to Old Four, six to five (the 16th). Correction in hand: a mark
  is held first and tested under the holder; *mine* before *yours.*

### M1.3 The night van

- The card went back signed on the 1st of the eleventh month and **stands unaltered.**
- **Two nights done:** the 9th (a string of three; three clean bags) and the 22nd–23rd of the
  first month.
- **The 23rd, the coal wharf below the Hythe:** a string of four with its skin laid on the
  ground for a cloth, which folds from the far edge and takes a rung home (**"a purse
  string"**). **Pike's hands were taken to above both wrists;** six weeks. Kiva said two words
  before a bag was sealed, a count late. Pru caught three rungs under Rhea's slate. Nothing
  tinned clean; 198 drams and a tin of skin red-tagged.
- **Standing line on Rhea Sorn's slate:** *The far edge: the second's. To say. Two words,
  once.*
- **Four nights remain** (two in the second month, two in the third): Varley, of Imrie's van,
  for the cloth; Pike rides as a voice; Kiva is second to Pike.

### M1.4 Other people

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **Mara Rowan** | Seven drums out by the 25th of the tenth. Twelfth sheet: no count shared with anything on the same water, pumps included. Left hand whole; reads banks from the 1st of the eleventh. Has Saltmere's failure, six minutes in a fitter's hand, and a tide table she cannot yet read. Has not answered Yeo. Knows of Hart. |
| **Orla Dane** | Told Kiva of Hart on the 24th of the tenth. Has not read the copy. Hart has written to Kiva and not to her. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Two cards and a list. The fitter with the watch at the Saltmere harbour pump house. Asked what Kiva's two fingers tell her; has been asked what his hand tells him. Not met. |
| **Pike** | Fifty-four. Hands in linen to the elbow. Right thumb slow since the autumn before last, in his own writing. |
| **Perrin Cade** | Ribs mended; his Hall's second team; captain, unwilling. |
| **Anwen Pryce** | Walks a wall forty paces. Keeps the wax. |
| **Pru Finch** | Second pin; caught on a company's floor; reserve. |
| **Selka Dray** | On two sticks. Unpersuaded. Marten captains Twenty-Eight. |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Card kept; slate changed. |
| **Dinah Coombe** | Hunt Marshal, Blaze; comes to Meridian in the second month; one floor a four at a dram a head. Not met. |

### M1.5 The grid

- **No drum on the Brickfields trunk is in step.** Drum twenty-nine's streets came off their
  boxes on the 6th of the eleventh month.
- A drum given a pump's count fell in with the pump through the water (drum nineteen, twice).
- **Saltmere tried the twelve sheets on the 20th and 21st of the eleventh month and failed:**
  the harbour's water is never still. Six cells went back in, at one to forty-one minutes;
  the long holds were all on the second day's hour. Six jars of plain spent; no more given.

### M1.6 Knowledge boundaries, changed

| Fact | Now also known to |
|---|---|
| Hart's first card and his request | Kiva (from Orla); Mara (from Kiva) |
| That Hart is a fitter at the Saltmere harbour pump house, by his hand on the list | Kiva, Mara |
| Hart's second card | Kiva, Mara, Orla (in the book) |
| That one line was left out of the copy | Hart, Orla (not which line, nor why) |
| **That Kiva dreams** | **Nobody new.** |
| Pike's thumb | Pike, Kiva, Rhea, Stroud |
| Nine red tags on the Halden license yard | The Rowan table of the 25th (Kiva, Anwen, Perrin, Jude, Pru, Mara). Not Birch. |

### M1.7 Dates ahead

Road south: inside thirty days of the 30th of the first month. The gate: Tuesday the 1st of
the third month. The Bench: Monday the 14th, **forty-four days** on. Rhea Sorn's card ends on
the 30th of the third month.

### M2.1 Kiva Rowan

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified;** one door declared at every board.
- **Hand to Ember: four crossings,** Circuit's mast, read by Noa, Orla present: the 1st (0.31), the
  8th (0.30), the 15th (0.31), the 22nd (0.30). **Four of Orla's six; not certified.** The Chair's
  ledger: the fifth and sixth at the Saltmere gate mast, read by N. Bexley, sponsor present, "one in
  seven days and not faster. Not certified until the sixth."
- **The hand sulks longer each time** (Aske, Monday at seven): knuckle/warm by noon; wrist/warm by
  four; an inch above the wrist/warm next morning; middle of the forearm/warm Wednesday the 25th at
  seven ("two nights"). After the fourth the card was held until warm. The two fingers as before.
- **Aske's card:** one crossing in seven days at the most; hand seen the next morning; "four of six."
  **Aske travels south with Seven by road.**
- **No Ember-to-still crossing, no stack, no three-door.** Three clean; not asked.
- **The two fingers** said *nothing* (and were believed, pulse 68–72) on a stone by the old kiln on
  Cowl Edge and on the Order's door jamb. The kiln husk sang the seventh reed.
- **The disk:** cold every time, including beside Clemency Thrale and through the fever.
- **The dream: recurred** on the Sag ferry, Friday the 27th, with a fever (112 by Pru) that broke at
  about four on Saturday. As on the page: no roof; nothing over her, enormous, paying attention;
  roads of unlike lights, some going out, some white; she was "not so high as she had been." **She
  told nobody.** Aske knows only that she has had such a fever before. **The dream is not in her
  book;** the fever is. "Unroofed Sky" has not appeared.
- **First time near a Glory master:** her three doors "lay down" in Thrale's presence.

### M2.2 Team Seven

- **Flask license in its own right,** the Marshal's floor, Thursday the 12th, one remark (a bag at
  sixty-two). *Under its captain's name.*
- **Lost the Marshal's lane to Halden License six to two** (the 10th); correction: the tap, the hand
  on the knee, the Kill guarding the pass. Kellan read it twice in six on the 16th.
- **The handed crossing** (Jude's name): Finch catch → "nobody" → Rowan carry (declared Ember) →
  "nobody" → Cade plant. Clean at a quarter count at both gaps on the 11th and before Coombe on the
  13th, on the Low Lane's unsanctioned mast. *Mine before yours* between unlike doors reads as one
  line (a stack) and is not used. **Not done live.**
- **Hunt at Cowl Edge** (19th–20th), own entry, under Cade, witnessed by Vane: a pack at Garside's mill
  (keeping the wheel's time; the hatch shut) and a lone husk in the old kiln. **59.2 clean; nothing
  plain;** the four's eighth, 7.40.
- **Injuries:** Talley, left shin burned (the 2nd) and left arm frosted to the elbow (the 20th, not
  taken; sling). Cade's ribs complain when he lifts.
- **Equipment:** Finch's own purse sheet (her name on the pole) frosted and replaced (2.40, no pole).
  Kiva had carried it to the mill as her second cloth, and it went over the race lip. Finch keeps the
  frosted sheet. Mrs. Pryce's grey double went over the third heap, and it is still the drill sheet.
  Perrin keeps Coombe's knotted rope.
- **The book:**

  bank, a floor, a crossing or a sale.*** Signed Cade and Rowan. The Order's 1.2 combed full on the
  third and silent on the seventh. That is the first comb since the thaw over *whole* plain ash. The
  soot-cut 2669 jar refused on the 24th was silent on the seventh and thin on the third.

### M2.3 The night van

- **Two nights, as voice and cloth:** Friday the 6th (a pack of three husks at the Brickfields tram
  sheds; three bags, 155 of 162) and Tuesday the 17th (a purse string in the level Corn Exchange yard;
  Rhea cut from behind standing on the skin's near end; three bags, 171 of 180). **Two nights remain**
  (the third month). Varley at the cloth; Pike rides as a voice; Kiva second to Pike, with the far edge.
- Rhea's *Before* column now carries the pack line; she asked for a copy of the pack sheet for Imrie's
  van.
- **Pike:** dressings to a band at each wrist by the 17th; fingers move a quarter; hands "by the fourth
  of next month, slow."

| **Clemency Thrale** | Met. Glory; one door; looks about fifty; forty jars of plain left after the gift; has not been past her gate in eleven years, out twice in her life; will not ask Kiva the four remaining questions. |

### M3.1 Kiva Rowan

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified**; one door declared at every board **by her captain's voice**.

  Meridian, two at the Saltmere gate mast, read by N. Bexley). **Two crossings certified**: Ember to still;
  hand to Ember. One in seven days at most.
- **Fifth** (Thu 3rd, 0.31) — cold to a hand below the elbow; warm Sun 6th at seven: **three nights.**
  **Sixth** (Thu 10th, 0.30) — cold to **the elbow, an ache in the joint** (noon and four; a hand below at
  ten; the noon check is told after Adlam's 11.30 visit and the wire *(repair)*); Aske on Friday at seven:
  "three nights to go" *(repair)*; warm **Mon 14th at seven: four nights.** Aske: the reach is at the elbow; he wants it warm on more than one morning before a seventh;
  **Thursday the 17th is the soonest.** Aske says the price before each crossing.
- **The notch** at the Saltmere gate mast follows a **single** cliff: **seven seconds** (fifth), **six**
  (sixth), on every pen. Meridian: eleven, after a second cliff only. Unexplained; "a coast thing."
- **The two fingers:** say *keeps time* everywhere in Saltmere; say **nothing at slack** (Gannet Creek; the
  basin grating) and report the slack's end before the water moves. **Hart's method begun:** same question,
  same hour (a quarter to seven), yes or no; first entry Sun 13th: yes.
- **The disk:** cold every time, including on the Proprietor's table. Fourth-column lines for both crossings.
- **Fever, Friday the 11th** after the Counting House: to 112, broke about three; **no dream.** Aske's card:
  *Fever, the second this year. Both near a Glory master, within the day.* Kiva said aloud, "Nothing came."
  Aske wrote a line on the card that she did not see.
- **Near the Proprietor her pulse falls to his count (57)** and her Ember and still door want to fall in; she
  held them shut.
- **The dream:** not recurred; **known to nobody new.** The ferry detail of lights "gone white" is **Kiva's
  alone** (not told to Mara). "Unroofed Sky" and "Breach" do not appear.
- **Refused** the Proprietor's request for the three-door on a Halden mast (price: forty drams to Seven's
  book; a seat on his roll at a Torch's wage). **Three doors: three clean; not done.**
- **Not done:** Ember-to-still crossing; stack; the handed crossing live (one yard rehearsal only).

Perrin's stance at Anwen's back, read at his request on the sanctioned gate mast with two readers (Tue 8th):

the difference. The lodger did." Entered by the Clerk **"in no circuit's column."**

### M3.4 Rules and rulings

- Dray's rule read the Ropewalk's lays as one line; Noa entered "a stack… an incident." **The Clerk's ruling
  (Tue 8th):** readings stand as readings; incidents are for the candidate's own circuit; **a lay is entered in
  the Saltmere column and is not an incident for host candidates**; Meridian's conditions bind Meridian; all
  readings under the rule are posted for the draft boards.

### M3.5 Saltmere and the harbour

- **The Hoop:** a grey ring that rises from the basin mud ten paces from the outfall grating at each spring
  low-water slack and lies down when it ends; inside it held water trembles **four and four at the bank's
  count**; while it stands the basin is otherwise **quiet by Hart's hand**. A hand high on the 26th of the ninth
  month; 4 ft 1 in a fortnight before; **4 ft 6½ in on the 8th and 9th of the third month.** Stood 6.12–6.33
  on the 8th (21 minutes; *repair*, was 6.35) and 6.13–6.33 on the 9th (20 minutes). Open owner item: the
  slack's start moves one minute between consecutive days (review C12).
- **The basin's slack lags the sea gate's table by twenty-three minutes.**
- **The Saltmere keeper's entry for the 11th of the third month, 2604** (found by Peverell): a ring a hand high at
  the outfall; a keeper's fever, two nights; "no roof… nothing over… not empty… not dark. Something was
  attending"; **the refinery's (Halden's, stepped) lamps on the Long Quay went white and stayed lit; the low
  town's went out**, half an hour. Known to Peverell, Mara, Kiva.
- **The prediction (sealed Fri 11th, received 10.07 at the Board's press, opens noon Wed 23rd):** slack at
  6.09 (6.07–6.12), 18–22 minutes; the ring at 4 ft 11 in (not less than 4 ft 10); water inside at the bank's
  count, 57 or faster; nothing else in the basin keeping any count. Hart and Rowan; witnessed Peverell, Yeo;
  watched written, Rusk.
- **The Step is quickening** (Adlam's bench books): a stepped row fell in at 48 a minute (2402, 2431), 50 in
  2604, 55 in the ninth month of 2680, **57** now; **the harbour bank beats 57.**
- **Coast craft on the page:** cut only at the slack; walls laid flat on water as a boom; purses with
  drawstrings; "a kiddle" (seven posts; works the flood and the ebb; stands at slack; can shed uncut).

  Thursday the 17th; Aske says the price first ("twice").** The two fingers as before.
- **New: right hand**, a burn of cold across the knuckles and the backs of the first two fingers (Wed, Mallow
  Creek, from a live skin on her Ember's fist). Greased, in linen; blister, peel by Sunday; "in a week you'll not
  know it." Writes left-handed meanwhile.
- **The pass in three used live for the first time** (Wed, Mallow Creek, on Yeo's word): Finch caught (her ward set
  as "the nearest thing," letting the sleeve fold onto it), Rowan carried six paces low on her declared Ember,
  **Trewin of the Tarn planted on the word**. It held. It saved Bridie Hebden; D. Penhale was already dead under
  the skin.
- **Yes/no log, a quarter to seven:** Mon the Inner Lock rail, yes; Tue a stave in a cooper's yard on Quay Street,
  yes; Wed the leg of the reader's desk at the gate, yes.
- **Her book:** a page torn from the back for the Bench rail (**the first ever torn out**; Pru keeps it). A new
  seven-column sheet: *Before. D. Penhale… Stopped at three.* Ten sheets of Pike's craft and the pass sheet lent to
  Ivey overnight Wed to copy.
- **The disk:** cold. **The dream:** not recurred; known to nobody new; no fever. White lights: hers alone.
- Knows the Proprietor wrote to Ysra Crest on Saturday the 12th for the sequence "in her hand" (Adlam, Mon).

### M4.2 Team Seven

- **Boards:** Bench **6th** (found 4:10; drained the jacket; stepped at 8:40, cells four and five, not the cell
  tuned; *(repair)* the Clerk's rule ranks held fours by find and stepped fours by length of hold; on the page
  Westerley second 5:20, the Tarn first 5:10 and Holloway second 5:00 are 7th–9th, and every other step on the
  page is 4:00 or sooner; the Bench rules include a minute at the rail "as at home"); Carry **1st** (3:08, clean; Cade up a twenty-two-rung ladder planting every rung on Rowan's *set*);
  Hunt **15th** (3.15, no remarks). **After two: third (43). After three: ninth (53), one mark off the roll of
  eight.**
- **Standings after three:** 1 Halden License 65; 2 Old Four 64; 3 Holloway first 59; 4 the Ropewalk first 58;
  5 Team Twenty-Eight 57; 6 Westerley first 56; 7 Westerley second 55; 8 Holloway second 54; 9 Team Seven 53;
  10 the Ropewalk second 46. The Lane, in brackets, from Thursday.
- **Perrin:** *(repair)* rehearsal climb of the Inner Lock ladder Mon at six, off his own thimble (not on the
  book). The seventh rib on the left **cracked across, not through** (Tue, the eleventh rung). Aske's card: no
  plant, no lift, nothing heavier than a cup, a fortnight at the least. Stood down from the Hunt; captains from the
  hard. Lifted Penhale's boards once at the spur, against the card.
- **Pru:** stood the Bench and the Hunt; **all ten fingertips frosted** (Wed). Called the far edge once on Seven's
  stow, *(repair)* at about a quarter past twelve.
- **Anwen:** drum; first wall laid on water (live). **Jude:** frosted left arm leaned on once at the Carry ("not
  worse"); no draft made.
- **The book:**

| Line | Drams |
|---|---:|
| Carried, Mon 14th | 4.16 |
| The Bench (Pryce 0.16, Finch 0.09, Cade 0.06) | −0.31 |
| The Carry (Cade 0.22, Pryce 0.09, Talley 0.04) | −0.35 |
| The Hunt, doors (Pryce 0.17, Talley 0.10, Finch 0.12) | −0.39 |
| The Hunt, the four's quarter of 12.6 clean | +3.15 |
| **Held** | **6.26** |
| **Sealed medical (W, plain)** | **2.0, unopened, bound** |

  Check: 4.16 − 1.05 + 3.15 = 6.26. Rowan's doors on her own thimble; Trewin's on the Tarn's book. Red-tagged from
  the Hunt: 47.1 (net's tin 9.1; near-post bag 38.0), entered by Halden's clerk as *claimed under the patent*. The
  sealed tin was asked about for Bridie's legs and **not wanted** (Aske: a burn, not a starved hand).

### M4.3 Deaths, losses and injuries

- **D. Penhale**, catcher, the Saltmere Company: **dead**, Mallow Creek, Wed 16th, about 12.45–12.50, under the far
  end of a stow's sleeve, making the book's near-edge recovery. The Board records it.
- **Maud Lyle**, the Ropewalk Hold: **ward gone, not shut**, entered by a lodger at the left ankle on Skerry Pill,
  about 11.26. Stance remains.
- **Bridie Hebden** (the Tarn, 15): both legs below the knee, a burn of cold; not to stand till Friday.
- **Trewin**: both feet to the ankle, frost. **The Tarn's catcher** (unnamed): ankle, Tue.

### M4.4 Other people

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **Yeo** | Officer at Mallow; told Bridie "by the book" it was ground; carries it. Decided the pass. *Remarks: none, to any four.* Wants Kiva's columns for her crew. *(repair)* Her slate records the turn "at a quarter to one, in the slack"; the seven minutes are in her words to Coombe and in Noa's ledger. |
| **Ivey** (new) | Company catcher to Yeo's closing crew; copying Kiva's columns; means to have a second. |
| **Coombe** | Drill at the causeway ("Who has your far edge?"). Had her page printed Wed night, headed with Penhale's name, by the Clerk's leave; out Thursday at five. *(repair)* Heard Penhale say "When it's printed, I'll read it" in the causeway queue; he said it again to Kiva in the channel ("I've told half the causeway so"). |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Hunt officer at Gannet Creek (*Lives: none*). With Yeo at the Company yard. One night left; card ends the 30th. |
| **Pike** | "He had nobody at his far edge." |
| **Mara Rowan** | Has **not touched a cell**. Decided Wed night: **thirty-six hands, taught by the 22nd, not hers**; Hart to teach the slack; she says *now* from the landing. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Showed Mara the springs dawn slack **held** (8th–11th: 6.12, 6.13, 6.13, 6.14) while the gate's table walked fifty minutes a day; at the neaps the basin walks with the table. Unexplained. |
| **Peverell** | Writing her fitters' names. Penhale's mother washes in Ropery Row. |
| **Juniper Carrow** | Shut her stance at the gate Wed at five: "I'm learning to declare." Edge only at the Lane. |
| **Kellan Renn** | Cut the lodger on no officer's word (Bray's remark, his). Found Halden's clerk entering every red tag as *claimed under the patent of 2391* ("nine-tenths of the Hunt"). |
| **Selka Dray** | Twenty-Eight won the Hunt. "That's three people… I've not changed my mind about you." |
| **Trewin** | The third in the pass, on the word. Carry 3:12, planting every step. |
| **The Proprietor** | Wrote to Crest (Sat 12th) for the sequence "on any terms she sets." |
| **Noa, Polglase** | A hair on all gate pens at 12.44 Wed with nobody on the mast; the Tarn's stow turned at 12.44, seven minutes before the water. No line drawn. |

deadline. **Wed 23rd: the prediction opens at noon (dawn slack 6.09).** Rhea's card ends on the 30th.

  1:20. Seven had the Purser out at 0:26.

  three-quarters after the first ten dry passes.
- **Skerry Pill:** Kellan sixty paces from Lyle. Kiva hears of Lyle on the cart, on the causeway.

## Movement Five block — chapters 33–40 (first draft, 2026-10-15)

Status: **from first-draft prose; reconciled 2026-10-15 to the consolidated same-author repair**
(`provenance/MOVEMENT-005-REPAIR.md`; lines it changed are marked *(repair)*). Rechecked (fresh-context, same family, job `01a5e43b`), two quotation marks fixed, accepted as editorial context 2026-10-15 (`provenance/MOVEMENT-005-RECHECK-CONFIRMATION.md`).

`provenance/MOVEMENT-005-CONTINUITY.md` (§15 lists the repair). If review or repair changes the prose,
this block is corrected from the surviving text. Span: 5 a.m. Thursday the 17th of the third month, 2681, to about
midnight on Sunday the 20th.

### M5.1 Kiva Rowan

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified**; one door declared at every lane by her captain's voice. **On the national
  license roll with Seven for the year.**
- **No crossing, no stack, no three-door.** Three clean, unchanged. Hand warm Thu, Fri, Sat (four, five, six mornings).
  **Aske said the price twice** (Thu 7 a.m., Sat 7.15 a.m.): the seventh past the elbow, a sulk of five nights,
  "not surprised by six"; one in seven days. **The week's crossing is kept for Wednesday the 23rd**; nobody has asked
  for it on the page.
- **Lost the exchange with Selka Dray by choice** (Sat, carry 3): declared "Ember, single, and no change"; told Dray to
  her face across the beds before the lane; shook her head at Perrin's open mouth on the count; downed by Dray's flat.
- **Right hand:** blisters opened Thursday (poles); re-wound; writes left-handed. Two fingers as before.
- **Fever, the third this year** (Sun ~2.50–11 p.m.; to 104) after the Proprietor (9 a.m.) and Thrale (2 p.m.). **No
  dream. "Nothing came," said aloud.** Aske: *Fever, the third. Nothing came. Said so.* (shown to her).
- **The disk:** cold, including beside both masters and through the fever.
- **Yes/no log:** Thu mooring ring by the Graving, yes; Fri Salt Hall gallery rail, yes; Sat Graving lip rail, yes; Sun
  loft bed rail, yes (written by Pru).
- **The dream is public** (Salt Hall Fri; Mara's sealed page opened and read in the Graving Sun; the Mercury). Her
  public list of occasions: at thirteen, Classification Day, **the Pit** (the page's occasion), the ferry *(repair;
  the Pit was missing)*. **The ferry's
  white lights told to the Board's commission at the Counting House** (Sun). **The colour with no word: Kiva alone; "not
  put on the table"; Mara has never been told it.** The *Witnessed* page has a third line (§M5.5).

### M5.2 The Lane and the Measure

- Lanes: **Thu 7–2 v the Tarn second four** *(repair; was 6–2: the third carry's down is now counted)* (stance; the
  hen; elbows); **Fri 4–5 v the Ropewalk first four** (Carrow, edge only, striking on Treen's "set"; "two places" lost
  the glass); **Sat 6–5 v Team Twenty-Eight** (Pru calls the pen; Kiva calls nothing). Seven: won two, lost one, **17
  for, 12 against (+5)** *(repair; was 16 for)*; **ninth in the Lane (16 marks).** Off the page, the unnamed eighth
  Lane four is +6 (was +5) and the Tarn second stays +10 (its off-page wins +15); see the continuity file §2.
- **Declaration rules now on the page** *(repair)*: a change between carries, declared to the box with nothing open in
  the pause, is entered on the card (Fri, stance → Ember); after "*and no change*" a captain may still call a change at
  the cost of a point to the other four, entered on both cards "that the word didn't hold" (Sat).
- **The Measure, read Sat 8 p.m., no board struck:** 1 the Ropewalk first 82; 2 Halden License 80; 3 Westerley first 79;
  4 Old Four 78; 5 Holloway first 77; 6 Holloway second 76; **7 Team Seven 69 (19 + 24 + 10 + 16)**; 8 Team Twenty-Eight
  68 and 9 Westerley second 68 (**tie broken by the best single place on any board: Twenty-Eight first on the Hunt**);
  10 the Ropewalk second 66; 11 the Tarn second 59. **On the roll by one mark.** Seven won the Carry outright.
- **The roll:** Ropewalk first, Halden, Westerley first, Old Four, Holloway first, Holloway second, Team Seven, Team
  Twenty-Eight. **Warranted** (Sun): a Barrel response at the harbour pump house at the dawn slack Wed 23rd; any roll
  four by name on the Board's warrant and ash; **none obliged**.
- Bracket draws: Thu by standing after three; Fri and Sat by lot at the pole (Sat's put back to 9 p.m. Fri).

### M5.3 Team Seven

- **The book:**

  Check: 6.26 − 1.11 = 5.15. Rowan on her own thimble. On the page: "A dram and eleven. Held, five and fifteen." The
  Thu/Fri per-head splits are the author's reconciliation. **The sealed tin was asked for by the Harbour Committee at the
  Board's sitting and refused by Perrin ("It's for a hand").**
- **Perrin:** captains from outside the pen. Aske's card: *A fortnight from Wednesday* (lifted at the spur against the
  card). Took Kiva's arm on the stair Sun night against the card; stopped by Aske.
- **Jude:** Aske's card: left forearm frost, mending, a stripe from the Carry's loop, will scar; *edge on the right fist
  only; nothing gripped with the left till Monday.*
- **Pru:** two fingertip caps torn Thursday (bled); re-capped.
- **Anwen:** taught Maud Lyle to lean from the floor (Thu).

### M5.4 Offers (draft boards; a card is not a seat)

- **Anwen:** Graywater, Doran Mace's ward line on the night van, her own place, from the 1st of the fourth month (card by
  R. Sorn). **Yes.** Told Kiva first.
- **Jude:** the Saltmere Company, Yeo's closing crew, from the 1st of the fourth month, six months. **Open** ("after
  Wednesday").
- **Perrin:** Halden Lift Yards ("the winner of the Carry") — **No, with thanks**; the Municipal Works trunk crew (Rusk),
  from the autumn when his card is up — **open** ("at home").
- **Kellan:** Halden technical office, junior assessor, with counsel's condition — **No**. Sunday night he holds a white
  Halden card whose contents are not on the page.
- **Kiva:** none; Rhea will ask on the 30th and write only if Kiva says yes.

### M5.5 Rulings, findings and lines

- **The Board's answer to the Harbour Committee (Fri):** "The Board does not stop banks. It does not keep them… The
  keeper keeps her house."
- **Halden's counsel withdrew the request to note the claim against the Hunt's bags "for the present."** The claim is for
  the Compact's court; Kellan's figures and Adlam's bench books sent with it.
- **The Clerk's finding (Sun):** a thing dreamed by Annick (2446–50), the Ferren and Saltmere keepers (2604), the
  Westerley keeper (2604), the Proprietor (2604 and eleven times), Thrale (three times), the Holloway lamp-man (2680),
  and Kiva Rowan (from a page that was shut). **"The Board will call it the unroofed sky… and it'll not say what it is."**
  The Mercury's late sheet: **THE UNROOFED SKY** (first appearance of the name).
- **The Chair:** answered the Proprietor in writing (*not to be asked… I set none*); entered Orla's request that her
  sponsor's line be struck when the certificate can stand in the candidate's own name (*on evidence*); wrote the third
  *Witnessed* line: *Put it on the table herself, under her own name, before anybody asked.*

### M5.6 The Slack: plain ash

- **The Order's thirty-six jars, given** (Thrale, in the Graving, Sun). The Order's cellar: **4 jars left** (44 → 41 → 40
  → 4). **Price said aloud:** the house breaks its only rule not about doors (never for a bank) and records it; four
  jars for every hurt hand on the road south; **the harbour bank's thirty-six cells never to be stepped again while the
  pump house stands, each its own count** — written in the keeper's book for 2604 by Peverell and signed by the Harbour
  Committee's chairman. Jars on a cart at the Graving's seaward end.

### M5.7 People

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **The Proprietor** | Testified under his name that he dreamed it (2604; eleven times since; last the 17th of the ninth month 2680, evening). His reading formed a year after the window, at twenty-seven *(repair)*. Read Annick in 2610 (the 2611 settlement followed). "Sure of my reading… not certain I'm right." Asked Kiva only "Were any of the lights white?" about the ferry (no source cited) *(repair)*. Left hand never lifted. |
| **Clemency Thrale** | In Saltmere from Sat 4.50 p.m.; lodges in the Company's watch hut at the Mole's end; an hour at a time in the town; testified (dreamed it three times: at nineteen; 2670; 17th ninth month 2680 at 2.20 p.m.); her reading: one door, one quiet thing, let nothing in that asks. Kiva's doors lie down at forty paces. |
| **Mara Rowan** | Took the national risk on her name (Fri). Sent the wires; broke the seal with Kiva; read the page aloud. "Don't be one engine." Has not touched a bank cell *(repair; she lays her reading hand on the training cells in the keeper's room)*; teaching the thirty-six in three twelves. Knows there was a white she wasn't told and a thing with no word she hasn't been told. |
| **Orla Dane** | One true sentence each with Hart (Sun 6.45 a.m.); nothing mended. Asked the Chair to strike her line when no longer needed. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Spoke under his name at the Salt Hall ("ruined… the one that reads"). |
| **Peverell** | "The keeper who let it be tried." Thirty-six names on fault paper, not hers. |
| **Kellan Renn** | Read his figures under his own name (1,106/1,102/4; 412.4/371.3/41.1; 109 drams 2391–2600; 1,175.1 in 2680). Halden second by two marks. |
| **Mr. Adlam** | Read the bench books (48, 48, 50, 55, 57). |
| **Noa Bexley** | Fourth column (ash). Wired the Holloway warden himself. Two hairs (12.44 Wed; 12.52 a.m. Thu, as a tear shut); no line. |
| **Selka Dray** | Read on the mast at her own request; beat Kiva in the exchange; "the fairest lane I've ever lost." Twenty-Eight on the roll by the tie. "Wednesday… I'll be there. On my feet." Not a friend. |
| **Juniper Carrow** | Won the Measure on one door, declared, "and no change", striking on Treen's "set". "If they try, I want to be on the wall." |
| **The Reader, L. (Lavinia) Arkwright** | In Saltmere from Sat 9.35 p.m. with Annick, Personal (custody) and Lumb's 2604 book. |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Wrote Anwen's card; will not write Kiva's before the 30th; "*Mine*", in her own head. One night left. |

## Movement Six block — chapters 41–48 (first draft, 2026-10-15)

Status: **from first-draft prose, unreviewed. The book's last movement and its ending.** Everything here is on the page of
`manuscript/chapter-41.md` to `chapter-48.md`. The full record is `provenance/MOVEMENT-006-CONTINUITY.md`. If review or repair
changes the prose, this block is corrected from the surviving text. Span: a quarter to seven on Monday the 21st of the third
month, 2681, to the evening of Thursday the 1st of the fourth month.

### M6.1 Kiva Rowan

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified** (no column made). **Certificate in her own name:** the Chair struck Orla Dane's
  sponsor's line on Wednesday the 23rd at four, *on evidence* (the first crossing below); the conditions stand as written;
  her book goes weekly to the Chair.
- **Crossings:** the seventh, Wed 23rd, 6.11.40, Ember to still, at the Graving's sill, read by N. Bexley at the Board's lane
  mast, declared by Cade at six: **inside her certificate.** The eighth, 6.14.50, Ember to still, at M3 inside the dock, **the
  second in the hour, against her healer's card**, the price said first by Aske (Monday; and at 6.13 inside a ten-count),
  declared by Cade, read; entered by the Chair *not an incident.* Both on the Board's ash.
- **The left hand: gone to the wrist from the 23rd (not cold; no feeling, no grip); the arm aching to the shoulder for days;
  three weeks; then as before, the two fingers as they were.** Right hand healed enough to write, badly.
- **Aske's card (Thu 24th): one crossing in fourteen days, at the most, for the year at the least; the hand seen the next
  morning; the three doors not on the card.**
- **Three doors: three clean.** Asked for the sequence by the Proprietor at 5.40 on the 23rd; **refused, with why** (second
  refusal in the book). No fourth.
- No fever after the Slack (*Two masters, and touching, and nothing*).
- **The disk: cold throughout**, including at 6.22 and at the hull. Fourth-column lines: *Cold. Both. As before.* (Wed);
  *The disk is cold.* (Thu 1st).
- **Yes/no log:** Mon *yes*; from Wed 23rd *no answer* (kept daily on Hart's method).
- Read a count through her still door for the first time (at the cut).
- **Seen to count, four and a rest, for nine seconds at 6.22** (Rhea's slate), with Thrale and the Proprietor.
- **The dream:** no recurrence. **The colour with no word: Kiva alone.**
- **The Charter Board's travelling commission:** *Rowan, K. Catcher. Caller of the pass in three…* under D. Coombe's hand,
  **from the 15th of the fourth month to the last of the ninth; then we look again, and either of us may say no**; on her two
  conditions (teach the call at every bank to two who are not her, leave it in seven columns, the next bank called by somebody
  else where possible; the dream is not the commission's, nobody asks). Signed K. Rowan and M. Rowan (guardian).
- Goes home on the Works' transit (Fri 2nd, the Board's fare).
- Her pass sheet's second edition: the first limit corrected (the caller need not have crossed; somebody must keep the count
  who isn't carrying).

### M6.2 Team Seven

- **The book (Finch's from Tue 29th):** held **5.15**, unchanged (rehearsal and Slack doors on the Board's ash).
  **Sealed medical, W, plain: opened Wed 23rd, 7.25, for L. Hosken's starved right hand (Aske); 1.2 used; 0.8 left, resealed,
  bound as before.** Signed Cade and Rowan.
- **The four go four ways:** Anwen to Graywater (left Thu 1st with the van); Jude to the Saltmere Company (yes, Wed 23rd, card
  signed Thu 24th, with the line on where every bag goes); Perrin to the Works trunk crew from the autumn (yes, by letter to
  Rusk, Sun 27th); Kellan to the Proprietor's own count office from the autumn (second white card, yes, Sat 26th). **Pru keeps
  Seven** (the book, the wax); no to Ivey.
- **Injuries:** Jude's right knuckles white (cold, not taken). Pru two caps torn. Perrin's card to the 30th (lifted nothing).

### M6.3 The Slack (Wednesday the 23rd)

- **The bank is out:** thirty-six cells each on its own count, not two alike; **each pump on its own six cells**, restarted
  one at a time 6.26–6.29. Low town wet 6.22–6.29 (four inches in Ropery Row). **Nobody died.**
- **The thing in the basin** (a cask: staves, chime, no head; the held water its head; 4 ft 11; count 57) lifted off the water
  in three handed passes (ring → M1 stake → M2 sill → M3, 45 paces inside the dock) and cut in the Graving on Rhea Sorn's slate
  on Kiva's count. The basin tear closed by Ivey's crew at 6.14. Students closed nothing.
- **Bad assumption found in time (Tue):** the plank must keep the thing's count (Dray's call) or it goes to the pumps.
  **Survived:** the Proprietor's pattern and his hand on the glass are one hold.
- **The Proprietor lifted his hand at 6.22.11;** the pumps stood; **every lamp on the coast to a point for nine seconds**
  (Saltmere; the coast's lamp offices to Westerley; three ports over the water); a hair on every pen at Noa's mast.
- **Thrale's ward across the dock mouth 6.15–7.04**, holding the purse and the flood; the gate seated on the flood at 7.04;
  74 minutes in the town.
- **The prediction (opened noon Wed):** 6.08 (in the limits; a minute off); 23 minutes, not clean (wrong); 4 ft 11 (right);
  57 (right); "lie down" not tested by design; "nothing else" wrong twice by the operation's own doing. Not on the page: the
  point of light.
- **Ledger:** the Order's 36 jars into 36 cells (cellar: **4**). The purse: **286.4 drams, all red**, held by the Board as
  evidence; **no claim; no share paid.** Thirty-six old cans boxed red as evidence. Kiva's crossings on the Board's ash.

### M6.4 Rulings

- **The Proprietor relinquished the foundation's claim on "all ash exhibiting the Step, however produced"** (fault paper,
  6.35 Wed, witnessed K. Renn; nine pages by counsel after; to the Compact's court). The foundation's own yards, refineries and
  lift yards stay stepped.
- **The grid order (Fri 25th):** every municipal bank on its own count and kept there; none stepped or joined to a stepped
  one; fallen-in banks taken out as plain allows; all plain declared to the Board within the year and allotted banks first and
  hands with them, no bank before a hand; a travelling commission under D. Coombe to every municipal bank within two years.
  **Price:** about a tenth of the light; a third again in fitters' wages (the towns'); the foundation's 112 municipal
  licences. **Not settled whether the Proprietor was right.**
- The Clerk records three people who have dreamed seen to count together (three written accounts); no line drawn.

| **Clemency Thrale** | Held the mouth; home on Friday's boat. "I counted. I don't know what." |

### M6.8 Book Two exit

The Measure read; the dream public with no agreed meaning ("the unroofed sky"); the harbour bank out and every municipal bank
ordered onto its own count; the claim relinquished; Kiva's certificate in her own name, her hand gone for three weeks, her
card at one in fourteen, three doors three clean, the disk cold; the four gone four ways; a hull with a light like no lamp in
Saltmere standing in from the south, not met.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — BOOK_MAP.md

# Kindling 2680, Book Two — *The National Measure* — book map

> Built by TAC

Status: **planned, not drafted.** This map states intended change, pressure, and payoff. It is
not a set of chapter contracts. The author owns scene selection, chapter boundaries, tactics,
conversation, humor, and the route through each movement. Entry state is `STATE_LEDGER.md`.
Everything here is `PLANNED` until surviving prose makes it canon.

## 1. Book promise

Kiva Rowan ended her first collegiate season seated, unclassified, with eight fingers that
report and a company's card that promises no seat. Book Two takes her two hundred miles to the
coast. She spends a winter with her hands dark, kneeling at a professional's hem with her
mouth shut. She trains for Riftspawn that no longer die by the book. She stands the National
Measure at Saltmere among twenty-four fours and schools that solved her problem a different
way. And she finds that the thing Meridian did once, by hand, to one bank on one hill, has to
be done on water that never lies still.

The title carries three meanings, and the book pays all three: the competition; the nation
counting what it has (keepers' books, banks, fevers, plain ash); and **the beat itself**, the
count of four that more and more of the country's ash is keeping.

## 2. The argument

Book One asked why the post is crooked and answered: keep the reason with the method. Book
Two asks what happens when a method has to **travel.** Mara's eleven sheets reach the coast and
fail there, because a harbour is not a storm drain. Kiva's three-door sequence cannot travel
at all; it lives in one damaged hand. What can travel is the part she has been made to learn
all winter: say what the piece is, who has it, and when it changes hands.

Her earned capability in this book is not a fourth three-door. It is the **handed crossing**
(working term): catch, carry, and plant done by three people with three unlike doors, with
one caller who has been inside both gaps and can say when the load passes. It costs almost no
ash. It costs trust, rehearsal, and a count that is not hers.

## 3. Complete ending

**The Measure is read in full.** No board is struck. The Ropewalk School's first four wins the
National Measure. Halden License is second. **Team Seven finishes seventh of twenty-four and
makes the national license roll, which takes eight, by one mark.** Seven wins one board
outright, the Carry, on Perrin Cade's back. Selka Dray beats Kiva in a lane, on a declared
count, with a needle on her, exactly as promised; Seven wins the lane on the other three.

Kiva loses that exchange by choice. One crossing in seven days is all her card allows, and by
then she knows what the week's crossing is for. She fights Dray on one door and says why
beforehand, to Dray's face.

**The dream becomes a public fact.** Before the Charter Board, Kiva has Mara's sealed page
opened: timed and sealed before she ever read Annick, it proves that her account was her own.
Beside it lie Annick's entry, the Ferren keeper's line of 2604, the Saltmere harbour keeper's
line for the same night, and the first returns of a national reading of remarks columns. Two
living Glory masters then say under their own names that they have dreamed it: Clemency
Thrale, Master of the Order of the Even Hand, and Ambrose Halden, the Proprietor, who was
alive on the night in 2604. A Board clerk borrows Annick's phrase, and the papers print **the
Unroofed Sky.** Nobody can say what it means. The two masters read it in opposite ways.

**The Slack.** At the dawn low-water slack of Wednesday the 23rd of the third month, a mixed
cohort from the Measure's roll and three professional crews take Saltmere's harbour bank of
thirty-six cells out of step. The water has to be still, and harbour water is still for about
twenty minutes. The thing standing in the basin has to come off the water first. It is the
first Barrel-class expression on the page, and it is keeping the harbour's time.

- **Students** stand the wall, carry, cut on a foreman's slate, and bag. Kiva calls three
  handed crossings in a row and tests every mark first.
- **When a link fails,** she supplies it herself: one certified crossing, on a sanctioned mast,
  read by the office's reader. It is the first decisive thing she has ever done that was
  entirely inside her certificate. **When a second link fails,** she makes a second crossing in
  the same hour, against her healer's card, with the price said first.
- **Professionals** close the tear. Fitters and keepers, under Mara Rowan, Jessamy Hart, and
  the harbour keeper, fill thirty-six cells on thirty-six counts.
- **The Proprietor** holds the harbour's pumps on his own pattern so that the low town does
  not flood. The operation cannot finish while he holds it, because his pattern *is* the Step.
  He lets go: of the bank, and in writing, of his foundation's claim on "all ash exhibiting the
  Step, however produced." **Master Thrale** holds a ward across the dock mouth and cannot move
  while she does.
- For some seconds two Glory fields from unlike traditions touch. **Every lamp on the coast
  shows a point of light.** Three people who have dreamed are seen to count together.

The bank comes out. The harbour's pumps stand, and the low town takes water. A professional
has died on this coast earlier in the book, and nobody dies at the Slack. The Charter Board
orders every municipal bank in the country put on its own count and kept there: a slower,
dearer, deliberately uneven grid. It is a real national answer and it costs light, money, and
an industrial house its oldest property.

**What it does not settle** is whether the Proprietor was right. He has believed for
seventy-seven years that stepped lamps are the ones that go white instead of out.

**Kiva's cost.** The hand is gone to the wrist for three weeks and returns as before. What
does not return is the rate: Aske's card now reads **one crossing in fourteen days.** Three
doors remain three clean. The fourth was asked for, by a Glory master, and she said no.

**The last of the third month.** Rhea Sorn and Kiva look again. Either may say no, and each
says no to something. Rhea names no seat; she writes Kiva's name on the Charter Board's
travelling commission, under another officer's hand, with *either of us* on it again. Kiva
takes it on two conditions of her own. Team Seven's four go four ways by their own choices.

**The last image.** Wires vouch what the ship's engineer said: ports over the water logged the
same point of light in the same minute. A hull stands in from the south with a light on its
bow that is like no lamp in Saltmere. Somebody else has dreamed, and has come to see who
answered. The disk is cold. Kiva writes that down.

## 4. Numerical and structural shape

- Six open-range movements of eight chapters; chapters 1–48; **285,000–315,000 words** unless
  scenes earn more. About 47,500–52,500 words a movement and 5,900–6,600 a chapter.
- **Viewpoint.** About 87% Kiva. About 13% across **five established lenses** at roughly 2.5 to
  3% each: **Anwen Pryce, Kellan Renn, Mara Rowan, Orla Dane, Rhea Sorn.** Cutaways are brief
  and occur where Kiva cannot witness an essential action. Perrin, Pru, Jude, Noa, Hart, and
  Dray develop through what Kiva sees. No new lens is planned.
- **Sentence and paragraph targets** (owner's formula, unchanged): mean 14.6 words; median 11;
  about 27.7% of sentences at five words or fewer; about 3.3% at forty or more; inferred
  paragraph median 18 words; scenes near 950 words, about 8.7 breaks per 10,000 words; Flesch
  Reading Ease near 72.3 and Flesch-Kincaid grade near 6.8. Measured after a movement exists.
- **Progression vocabulary** near 58 hits per 10,000 words across the book, with the opening
  third (Movements One and Two) at about 1.7 times the density of the rest.
- **Development cadence.** A Kiva realization or decision about every 2,200 words, spread
  evenly with a slight middle bump. Ten supporting people get small beats; none takes the
  secondary story.
- **Action.** Every movement has a developed physical set piece and a learning encounter.
  There is no quiet movement.

## 5. Calendar

| Span | Movement |
|---|---|
| Monday the 17th of the tenth month, 2680, to Saturday the 30th of the first month, 2681 | One |
| The second month, 2681 (Sunday the 1st to Monday the 30th), ending on the road south | Two |
| Tuesday the 1st to Sunday the 13th of the third month: Saltmere, the gate fortnight | Three |
| Monday the 14th to Wednesday the 16th: the Bench, the Carry, the Hunt | Four |
| Thursday the 17th to Sunday the 20th: the Lane, the hearing, the reading | Five |
| Monday the 21st of the third month to Thursday the 1st of the fourth: the Slack and after | Six |

The National Measure runs **Monday the 14th to Saturday the 19th of the third month, 2681.**

## 6. Six movements

### Movement One — The Second (chapters 1–8)

**Entry pressure.** Stood down from every door for eleven weeks, Kiva has to be worth
something with her hands dark. A method that worked once is being carried to seven drums and
then to the coast. A company's card says she may watch.

**Engine.** The handoff list becomes a craft. Orla tells her about Hart, and Kiva decides what
of her book a stranger may read. The new year brings the first re-reading of her figures
since Classification Day. Two nights as Pike's second teach what a professional catcher does
that he never says aloud, and where twenty-six years of it stop being enough.

**Action and turn.** Drum work under Mara. A professional call where the book answer fails
against a mixed expression and a professional is hurt. Kiva's two dead fingers are tested by
dark-hand work at a right-hand corner. The warrant for Saltmere is posted, and it is dearer
than Kellan's sum. Saltmere answers Mara's sheets: tried, and failed, on water that moves.

**Exit.** Kiva has started writing down Pike's method in seven columns. Kellan has chosen his
four. Seven's entry is signed under a captain nobody expected. After a fifteen-dram warrant,
the book has 10.53 drams left; about 5.8 of that is already spoken for by the planned
hand-to-Ember crossings and Coombe's floors, before any call reserve. The only plain
ward-trace is the sealed 0.8 medical reserve. The Measure has four boards, one of them a live
hunt.

### Movement Two — Something Quiet (chapters 9–16)

**Entry pressure.** The second month to make a four that can stand a licensed hunt against
things its kill drills were not written for: thirty days until the road south and forty-four
days from Movement One's last day to the first board, on a book whose available ash is
already spoken for.

**Engine.** Several connected chapters of training for stronger and more varied spawn under
Hunt Marshal Dinah Coombe, a Blaze who has run mixed fours on the coast: packs that keep a
count among themselves; a hem that folds like a ward; a form that takes a stacked door as an
invitation. Kiva spends her weekly go certifying a second crossing, hand to Ember, and the
hand sulks longer each time. The handed crossing is assembled from parts the reader already
owns: the plank, Pru's fold, the Line's old word, and Pike's unwritten habits.

**Action and turn.** A learning fight against Halden License in a prepared lane, where Kellan
stands opposite. Seven's first licensed hunt in its own right, in the hills round the Sag,
looking for something quiet enough to refill a medical reserve. They kill well. Nothing comes
up plain. The only plain ash on the road south is in the house of the Even Hand, whose door
has been on the latch since the summer.

**Exit.** Seven has its Flask license and a method with three weeks of rehearsal in it. Kiva
has stood near a Glory master for the first time, and the dream has come back, with a fever,
on the ferry.

### Movement Three — Water That Keeps Time (chapters 17–24)

**Entry pressure.** Saltmere is proud of its harbour bank. Thirty-six cells in step have not
dropped a stroke in six months, and the Works' reader from Meridian is a guest telling her
hosts that their best machine is a hazard.

**Engine.** Other schools solve the same problem in ways that look incompatible. The Ropewalk
School has a column for two doors; it teaches them **laid**, open together and agreeing, which
Meridian calls a stack and forbids. Dray's sealed mast rule makes the host's best candidates an
incident, and Meridian is disliked for it. Jessamy Hart turns out to be the man who can tell
the Works when the harbour is still. The harbour keeper's book has an entry for the 11th of
the third month, 2604. Something stands in the basin at each spring tide, and stands a little
taller each time. Hart and Mara seal a prediction.

**Action and turn.** The gate fortnight's seeding rounds. A coast night on Graywater's van,
called south under a Charter Board warrant. The Proprietor asks to see the plate and is
answered by a watch's vote. He then asks Kiva for the three-door, on a Halden mast, for a
price. She says no.

**Exit.** Seven is seeded in the middle of twenty-four. The prediction is under seal. Orla and
Hart have spoken once, badly.

### Movement Four — The Longshore (chapters 25–32)

**Entry pressure.** Three boards in three days, and the third is alive.

**Engine.** The Bench, without Kiva. The Carry, which is Perrin's. The Hunt, on the tidal
flats, scored on clean yield after shares, with every team's ash combed for the Step.

**Action and turn.** The Hunt is the book's midpoint set piece. Coast forms work the tide. A
laid door is entered, as Coombe's drill said it could be: Maud Lyle of the Ropewalk loses a
door, perhaps for good. A professional catcher of the Saltmere Company is killed making the
recovery that the book prescribes. The handed crossing is used for the first time against
something live, by Seven and one borrowed still-door from another circuit, and holds.

**Exit.** After three boards Seven stands ninth, a place off the roll. The Ropewalk's captain,
Juniper Carrow, has shut one of her own doors and is learning to declare. The Measure goes on
under a death.

### Movement Five — A Declared Count (chapters 33–40)

**Entry pressure.** The sealed prediction falls due on the 23rd, four days after the final.
Kiva has one crossing a week. The lane against Dray and the Slack both want it.

**Engine.** The Lane, over three days. The Charter Board's hearing on the three sheets. Draft
boards. Kellan's numbers, which run against his own house. Kiva's choice to put the dream on a
table.

**Action and turn.** Developed lane fights against rivals who have adapted: Carrow on one
door; Dray on her feet. Kiva loses the exchange she wanted, on purpose, and Seven wins the
lane. The sealed page is opened. Two Glory masters testify. The Measure is read.

**Exit.** Seven is seventh, on the roll by one mark, warranted for the Slack. Offers are on the
table for Anwen, Kellan, Jude, and Perrin. Everybody now knows that Kiva dreams.

### Movement Six — The Slack (chapters 41–48)

**Entry pressure.** A scheduled operation with a twenty-minute window, planned by people who
disagree, against a thing that has grown at every spring tide since the autumn.

**Engine.** Rehearsal, in developed scenes, with one bad assumption found in time and one that
is not. The Graving, a dry dock turned arena, becomes the purse that catches the ash when its
gate is shut.

**Action and turn.** The Slack, as section 3 gives it: three handed crossings, two failed
links, two crossings of her own, a master who has to let go, a point of light in every lamp.

**Exit.** The complete ending of section 3.

## 7. Kiva's progression ladder

| Rung | Where | What it costs or proves |
|---|---|---|
| Dark hands: a corner, a voice, a named handoff | One | Usefulness without a door. The two fingers are tested at a right-hand corner. |
| The re-reading | One | First measured figures since the spring of 2680. Draw falls a long way from 3.1 and is still poor. Ward Hold rises most. The rank frame holds **Flame** on one declared door and goes to dashes with two. No first door. Exact figures are the author's, inside those bounds. |
| A second's eye | One | She can say what a master did that he did not say. Written in seven columns. |
| Single doors relit | One, Two | Each door under Aske's eye before any crossing. |
| Hand to Ember, six times | Two | Certified by Orla on the sixth. About 1.8 drams and six of her weekly goes. |
| The handed crossing, rehearsed | Two | Three people, three doors, one caller. Works on a yard. |
| Flask license, in the four's own right | Two | A licensed hunt without a company officer's name on the entry. |
| The handed crossing, live | Four | Holds against a coast form with a borrowed still-door. |
| A crossing declined | Five | She fights Dray on one door. A loss chosen for a reason said aloud. |
| A crossing inside her certificate, in the field | Six | Ordinary terms, at last. |
| A second in the same hour | Six | The card becomes one in fourteen days. |
| The national license roll; the commission | Six | A place earned by a four and a method, not by three doors. |

**Three doors stay at three clean.** The sequence is asked for twice in this book and refused
twice. If drafting makes a fourth unavoidable, it must be bounded by a written direction, an
officer's hand, and the price said first by her healer, it must cost something that does not
come back, and it must be raised with the owner before it is written.

## 8. Team and ash ladder

- **Entry:** held 25.53; sealed 0.8; no warrant posted.
- **The warrant** is planned at three drams a head for five heads (Kiva, Anwen, Perrin, Jude,
  and Pru as reserve): 15.0. Kellan's place is paid by the Halden license four.
- **Planned spends before Saltmere:** six hand-to-Ember crossings, about 1.8; Coombe's floors,
  about 4.0; a call reserve; one crossing a week kept for Ember to still when the hand allows.
  The book runs short in the second month, on purpose, and the licensed hunt has to pay.
- **Plain ash is the scarce thing.** Every natural yield now carries the Step in trace. Taking
  a bank out of step needs a jar of plain ash a cell. Seven's eight-tenths of sealed plain
  ward-trace becomes worth more than the rest of its book, and an honest man offers to buy it.
- **Exit:** the author reconciles every dram. No free practice fuel. The red bags stay
  evidence.

## 9. Relationship arcs

| Pair | From | To |
|---|---|---|
| Kiva and **Pike** | Second and master; mouth shut | He is hurt and becomes a voice at her hem. Each has been the other's second. |
| Kiva and **Rhea Sorn** | A bounded card | Rhea takes a count from her catcher at the Slack and gives her name to another officer. |
| Kiva and **Orla** | Sponsor's line | Orla asks the Chair to strike her own line. Kiva's certificate stands in Kiva's name. |
| Kiva and **Mara** | Guardian who will not own the choice | Kiva has to watch Mara take the national risk, and not own that either. The seal is broken by both. |
| Kiva and **Anwen** | Reconciled, careful | Equals who are about to work in different places. Anwen takes a seat of her own. |
| Kiva and **Perrin** | Envy said aloud | He captains, wins a board, and finds that being envied is worse. |
| Kiva and **Kellan** | A blade told where he is wanted | He stands for Halden License, fights her, and then puts his numbers where they hurt his house. |
| Kiva and **Pru** | Catcher and cord | Pru catches a national board in her place and is not grateful, only good. |
| Kiva and **Selka Dray** | Unpersuaded | Dray wins her lane, calls the count again on her feet, and is still not a friend. |
| Kiva and **Jessamy Hart** | A card she has not seen | Two people with a bad hand and a book between them. He teaches her what it is for. |
| **Orla** and **Hart** | Twenty years of not writing | One true sentence each. No absolution. |
| Kiva and **Juniper Carrow** | The nation's best, on a doctrine Meridian forbids | Each uses a piece of the other's method at the Slack. |

## 10. Action, training, and learning promises

1. **Dark-hand work under load** (One): a drum that will not come out; a corner at a hem.
2. **A professional call that exceeds the book** (One), developed, with the correction shown.
3. **Connected spawn-training chapters** (Two): at least three new behaviors, each met first
   as a failed attempt, then as a drill, then under pressure.
4. **A learning fight against Halden License** (Two) that leaves a correction Seven can try
   again.
5. **A licensed hunt in the hills** (Two): terrain, yield, grading, and a disappointment.
6. **Seeding rounds against an unlike doctrine** (Three): the lay beats Seven fairly.
7. **The Hunt on the Longshore** (Four): the long set piece of the middle. A death.
8. **The Carry** (Four): Perrin's board, a full scene.
9. **The Lane against Carrow, and against Dray** (Five): two long fights, each changing
   problem by problem.
10. **The Slack** (Six): rehearsal, operation, and aftermath.

Fights follow the seat's rule: terrain, starting positions, reach, objectives, injuries, and
limits are set privately first; the opponent learns too; costs carry forward.

## 11. Evidence ladder for the filter and the dream

| Movement | What accumulates | What the public calls it |
|---|---|---|
| One | No plain ward-trace in Meridian. A mixed expression on a night call. Saltmere's failure. | A gifted class; a bad winter. |
| Two | Nothing quiet in the hills. The Order's cellar. Kiva's dream returns near a Glory master. | A bad rift season. |
| Three | A second keeper's book for the 2604 night. The basin at each spring tide. The notch is shorter on the coast than at home. | A coast problem. |
| Four | A laid door entered. Coast forms on the tide. | The worst Hunt in memory. |
| Five | The sealed page. Two living masters. Returns from remarks columns in other cities. Old Halden bench books that give the Step a slower count than today's. | **The Unroofed Sky.** |
| Six | A coast-wide point of light. Wires from over the water. | A national fact, with no agreed meaning. |

What the book establishes by its end: the Step is the mark that the rising pressure leaves in
ash; prepared people and rifts are answering the same pressure; it is quickening; it is not
only this country. What it does not establish: what the pressure is, why it keeps a count,
what the gathering is for, or what happens when the roof is off.

## 13. Reserved (do not spend in Book Two)

The full loss of the filter. Pure Glory presence. The cause of the Fall. The disk's origin and
purpose, and the first bearer's book. Why the pressure keeps a count. Who or what is "paying
attention." The color with no word. Whether Hesper Annick, who walked out in 2461, died. Any
returning figure from the first Kindled trilogy. A doctrine that makes the schools obsolete.
The resolution of anything international. The registry word "Breach," which stays unused
unless the owner asks for it.

## 14. Movement packet rule

Each movement gets one compact brief: entry state, pressure, a few consequences, boundaries,
output paths, and room. `packets/MOVEMENT-001.md` is written. Later packets are written from
the surviving prose of the movement before, never from this map alone.

| **Orla Dane** | Accountable sponsor and teacher; Torch; twenty years on the Line; bad knee | To do right by Hart without using Kiva to do it | Tells Kiva about the card herself, early. Meets Hart and is not forgiven. Asks the Chair to strike her own line once it is no longer needed. |

## Places and bodies new to Book Two (planned names)

- **The Ropewalk School**, Saltmere: the host circuit's leading school. Teaches two doors
  **laid** together. Its circuit has a column for it.
- **The Graving:** Saltmere's national floor, a dry dock turned arena, with a gate that keeps
  the harbour out.
- **The Longshore:** the tidal flats where the Hunt is held.
- **The Saltmere Company** (green; founded before 2362) and **Graywater** (slate blue) are
  Book One's. **The Charter Board** is Book One's.

## Viewpoint

About 87% Kiva. Five established lenses share about 13%: **Anwen, Kellan, Mara, Orla, Rhea.**
Each cutaway shows an action Kiva cannot see. Perrin, who had a lens in Book One, is seen
through Kiva in Book Two; his board is hers to watch.

## Development budget

The lead has a steady realization or decision about every 2,200 words. Small, independent
beats are spread across **Anwen, Perrin, Kellan, Pru, Jude, Noa, Orla, Mara, Rhea, and Pike,**
with Dray, Hart, and Carrow as the rivals and mirror who adapt. No single friend or mentor
takes the secondary story.

## Voices to keep

Anwen owns "I want it said" and "What's behind it?" Perrin minds at length and is funny about
it. Kellan does sums aloud and says "I'd expect nothing else." Pru says a big thing and goes
scarlet. Jude scratches his jaw and asks what ash is for. Mara is dry and exact. Orla is rough
and brief. Rhea allows herself one piece of advice a season. Pike nearly smiles. Dray's face
is a shut door. Aske counts so that others need not.

## Name and narration checks

- Avoid aural collisions with Book One's cast and with Callie, Jab, Toren, Rook, Elias, Vera,
  Dessa, Milo, Wyck, Senna, Dee, Katori, Tamar, and Meridian. "Keld" is taken (Idony Keld).
- No name or near-homophone from the research corpus.
- Checked and unused at planning: Coombe, Dinah, Carrow, Juniper, Lyle, Maud, Ambrose,
  Clemency, Thrale, Peverell, Ropewalk, Graving, Longshore.
