## PROJECT EVIDENCE — UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md

# Kindling 2680, Book Two — universe bible

> Built by TAC

Status: **planning.** This file does not replace
`books/book-2680-opening/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md` or `universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md`; read both. It
restates the rules Book Two leans on and adds only what Book Two needs. Three labels are used
and kept apart:

- `ESTABLISHED` — on the page of Book One or locked in its bible.
- `PLANNED` — intended for Book Two. Not canon until surviving prose says it.
- `RESERVED` — true or possible in the series, and not to be spent in Book Two.

## 1. Rules that do not move (`ESTABLISHED`)

- One source, three traditions: Ember, rift-granted magic (the hand door: Ward, Sustain), and
  internal cultivation (the still door). The soul fills. Ash is ownerless, finite fuel.
- The rank ladder: Kindled, Ember, Flame, Fire, Torch, Blaze, Glory. Rank and breadth are one
  axis; skill and control are another. Hold, Draw, Call, yield, grade, resonance, license,
  and standing are separate measures, and none pays for another.
- **Draw** is ash spent on a piece of work over the book expectation for it: 1.0 is standard,
  lower is better.
- A kill does not raise rank. Ash funds the next attempt. Learning is what makes it survivable.
- Ash is recorded by **yield** (Handful, Flask, Barrel, Well), **grade**, and **resonance.**
  The comb's third reed sings for ward-trace (W). The seventh (I) is cut to the Step.
- Glory stops ordinary aging, is rare, and cannot be granted, bought, or stolen. It widens
  what one person can hold: throughput, duration, precision. It never makes ash free.
- Riftspawn expressions echo worked powers with nobody home to direct them. Spawn anatomy
  as trained: breath; posts or load paths; knots; a cord; a hem; the tear; closure.
- Students close no tear. Licensed crews do. Radiation work and licenses not held are not
  taken over in a crisis.
- Classification is physical: rigs, masts, pens, tapes, projected tables, human rulings. No
  private overlay.
- **Doors, for Kiva.** A competition is all doors open and fighting. A stack is two open
  together and agreeing: soft, cheap, and open to anything near; forbidden to her. A sequence
  shuts each door before the next opens, and each gap costs about a quarter of a dram with
  nothing open. A crossing is one gap. Three doors are two gaps.
- **The disk** does not teach, speak, map, choose, open a door, or answer. Two bearers have
  written *warm, once,* after a first crossing, and *cold* ever after.
- **The dream** is a fever, not a prophecy that can be consulted. It cannot be summoned. Each
  recurrence has a bodily cost. Triggers on record: fever, ash overload, filter instability,
  close contact with Glory.
- **The Step** (Halden's foundation patent, 2391): a row of cells salted late with one pinch
  of ash already made to keep a beat thereafter beats as one and can be driven by one
  operator. Used in Halden's yards, refineries, lift yards, and big tram sheds. Pump houses
  and tenement boxes were never meant to be stepped. The claim covers "all ash exhibiting the
  Step, however produced." The caution: "Not to be introduced to an unstepped bank under
  load." Before the patent the Wren House called it *counting ash.*
- Shown, not explained, in Book One: stepped pumps make water rise and fall on a count of four
  and four; a cell joined to a stepped bank by nothing but that water goes into step; a tear
  wants "the thing that keeps time" and not the bright thing; a string with an arm in the
  water is itself a pump; cut cords spill open ash that can cook a bank.

## 2. The country (`PLANNED`, built on `ESTABLISHED` pieces)

- **The Charter Board** (`ESTABLISHED` as a name) licenses rift companies nationally, keeps the
  national roll, and runs the National Measure. It can ask a nation to look at its banks. A
  Circuit cannot.
- **Six circuits** send their first company of four fours each: **twenty-four fours.** Meridian
  sends Halden License, Old Four, Team Twenty-Eight, and Team Seven. Saltmere hosts. The other
  four circuits are named as the lanes need them.
- **The National Measure,** Monday the 14th to Saturday the 19th of the third month, 2681.
  **Four boards,** as at the Regional, each marked by place, twenty-four down to one, and
  added. The Hunt stands where the Regional's Call stood:
  1. **The Bench** — banks of cells with a fault planted; find, quench, re-tune, restart.
  2. **The Carry** — rescue and extraction against a clock.
  3. **The Hunt** — a licensed live hunt on tidal ground under company officers, marked on
     clean yield after shares, less every remark. Students close nothing.
  4. **The Lane** — the declared-role lane, as at home: three for the litter over a team's own
     line, one for a down, two halves a down. Run as a bracket over three days.
- **The national license roll.** The first eight fours are licensed for the year to stand
  Barrel-class responses under a company officer. Placement therefore decides who is sent for.
- **Gate rules.** Every dram is combed. Dray's rule arrives under seal: any candidate with
  more than one door is read from a sanctioned mast by a reader of the Meridian office. The
  host's laid candidates fall under it.
- **The warrant.** A circuit's fours travel on their own books. Planned at three drams a head.
- **Draft boards** sit through the Lane days. A company may card a student; a student may say
  no. A card is not a seat.
- **Travel.** Two hundred miles: the road round the Sag, a ferry, a river, and the coast boat.
  Station transit exists and is too dear for a student four.

## 3. Saltmere (`PLANNED`)

- A harbour city. The Saltmere Company's green is `ESTABLISHED`, as is its harbour bank of
  thirty-six cells, in step since twenty past two on the 17th of the ninth month, 2680.
- **The Ropewalk School** leads the host circuit. It teaches two-door candidates to open both
  doors together and twist them to agree: **a lay,** *laid* doors. It is what Meridian calls a
  stack. It is cheap, quick, and soft, it wins lanes, and its circuit has a column for it. It
  is not foolish. It was built against people and against the spawn of forty years ago.
- **The Graving:** a dry dock roofed with tiers. A floating gate keeps the harbour out. With
  the gate shut and the sluices closed, the dock is a basin of still water whatever the tide
  does outside.
- **The Longshore:** miles of tidal flat and creek where the coast's tears open.
- **The harbour.** Pumps keep the low town dry. The harbour's water moves twice a day and is
  still only at slack, for about twenty minutes. The Ferren method needs still water. Mara's
  sheets were tried at Saltmere in the winter and failed for that reason.
- **Many in Saltmere like the stepped bank.** It has not dropped a stroke. One operator can
  drive it. That is a fair position held by sensible people.

## 4. Spawn in a national season (`PLANNED`)

All new behavior obeys trained anatomy and combines or redirects it. None is a bigger Flask.
Working descriptions follow; the crews on the page give the names, as Book One's did.

- **Packs.** Husks that keep a count among themselves and move on it. Cutting one out of time
  breaks the pack for a few counts. Cutting in time does nothing.
- **A folding hem.** A hem that folds from its far edge, as a catcher's ward does, and so
  comes off a purse sheet.
- **An entering form.** A cord that goes to an open stack as a string goes to water that
  keeps time. A sequenced door gives it nothing to enter. A laid door is an open door.
- **Coast forms.** Things that stand in tidal creeks and work the flood and the ebb, where no
  sheet can be laid and ash goes into the water.
- **The first Barrel.** The expression in the harbour basin, grown at each spring tide. Its
  shape is the author's. It is not the Pit's square and not Stopgate's string abreast.
- **Yield** is higher on average and nearly all of it reads I above trace. Rich ash that
  cannot go on a floor, into a pump house, or into a medical tin.

## 5. Ash in a national season (`PLANNED`)

- **Plain ash is disappearing.** Natural yield carried the Step in trace by the spring of 2680
  (`ESTABLISHED`: "it's all had the hair since the thaw"). By the winter no fresh kill reads
  plain, in Meridian or in the hills.
- **Plain ash is what un-stepping needs:** a jar a cell, of ash that "has never heard a beat."
  Old stock is finite: reference shelves, wardens' winter stores, sealed medical reserves, and
  the cellars of an Order that never stepped anything.
- So the price of ash turns over. The richest red bags are close to worthless for safe work,
  and eight-tenths of a dram of plain ward-trace is worth an argument.
- A healer can ease a **starved** hand with plain ward-trace and cannot ease one that is
  **turned out** (`ESTABLISHED`). Stepped ward-trace is no use for it.

## 6. The handed crossing (`PLANNED`; the working term may be replaced on the page)

- **What it is.** Catch, carry, and plant, each done by a different person through one door,
  with a caller. A hand door lifts and folds. An Ember door takes the folded load across. A
  still door plants it. Between people there is no quarter-dram gap, because nobody shuts and
  opens.
- **What it costs instead.** For an instant at each pass the load belongs to nobody. A pass
  called half a count wrong drops it or nips someone (`ESTABLISHED`: Pru's fingers). Each
  holder must take a count that is not theirs. It needs rehearsal with those exact people.
- **Why it was not already done.** Schools teach one door and declared roles; a pass between
  unlike doors belongs to nobody's curriculum. The Home Line had a word for it once. A
  single-door fighter cannot feel the far side of a gap. Somebody who has been through both
  can call it.
- **Limits.** It is slower to set than a three-door sequence. It fails if any one of three
  people is hurt, late, or unwilling. It cannot be done by strangers. It does not close a
  tear, kill anything, or mend a bank.
- **Kiva's part** is the call, the ground test, and the written columns. Under her certificate
  she may also stand as one link by making one declared crossing on a sanctioned mast.

## 7. What Book Two establishes about the Step, the filter, and the dream (`PLANNED`)

Each line is to be earned by evidence on the page and said by somebody who can be wrong.

1. **The Step is a mark.** It is what rising pressure from beyond the filter leaves in ash.
   Halden did not invent the beat in 2391; the foundation found counting ash, learned to
   induce it, and built an industry on it.
2. **Tears come to it.** A stepped bank is a worked pattern keeping time at scale, and spawn
   expressions echo worked patterns. That is why a hold stood, and why a string went to the
   drain.
3. **People and spawn are answering one pressure.** Noa's columns get a fourth, for ash, and
   it rises with the other three.
4. **It is quickening.** Old Halden bench books give the Step a slower count than today's
   four and four. (The figures are the author's, after reading the patent scene in Book One.)
5. **It is not uniform.** The notch that follows Kiva's cliff by eleven seconds at Meridian
   follows it sooner on the coast. Nobody can say what that distance is a distance to.
6. **The dream is shared.** Annick (Glory, 2431; her entry undated); the Ferren keeper and the
   Saltmere keeper on one night in 2604; Kiva in 2676, proved independent by a sealed and timed
   page; Ambrose Halden in 2604 and since; Clemency Thrale; and returns from other cities'
   remarks columns.
7. **A T-0 can be local and can be wide.** When two Glory fields from unlike traditions touch
   at the Slack, every lamp on the coast shows a point of light, and ports overseas log the
   same minute.
8. **The readings disagree.** Halden: step the country, so that its lamps go white and not
   out. The Even Hand: one door, one quiet thing, and let nothing in that asks. The Works and
   the Line: whatever is coming, do not be one engine tonight. Book Two acts on the third and
   does not disprove the first.

## 8. Reserved truths (`RESERVED`)

- The complete loss of the filter, and Glory presence, belong to Book Three.
- What the pressure is; why it keeps a count; what is "paying attention."
- The cause of the Fall. The Makers.
- The disk's origin and use, the nine rings, and the first bearer's book. In Book Two the disk
  is cold, including beside two Glory masters.
- The color with no word. Only Kiva and Annick's page have it.
- Whether Hesper Annick died in the winter rifts of 2461.
- What the 2446 and 2604 lamp nights were *caused* by.
- Kiva's classification. No column is made for her in Book Two.
- Whether three doors can be made safe or taught. They are not done in Book Two.
- Any returning figure from the first trilogy.
- A single doctrine. The lay keeps its uses. The Order keeps its door. The handed crossing is
  one method.
- Anything settled between nations.

## 9. Guardrails for the author

- Every climax capability is shown and priced earlier.
- A Glory master on the page has a limit the reader can see: a place they cannot leave, a
  time they cannot exceed, a thing they must hold or let go. No Glory master does a student's
  or a fitter's work.
- No fingers heal. No rank, ash, device, or result restores them.
- No institution is a villain. The Halden Works and the Municipal Works stay distinct.
- Ledgers reconcile to the hundredth.
- Dream knowledge moves only by a decision made on the page.
- Nothing from the research corpus: no names, terms, scenes, or lines.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — packets/MOVEMENT-006.md

# Movement 006 — The Slack

- Series: Kindling 2680. Book Two, *The National Measure.* Public byline: Monroe Jackson.
- Seat: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor 1.3.0. Requested author: Claude Opus 5.5.
- Chapters: **41–48, one continuous creative run. This is the book's last movement and its ending.**
  This is one movement brief, not eight chapter cards. The author owns scene order, chapter boundaries,
  tactics, discoveries, humor and the route through the pressure, including how the eight chapters divide
  between rehearsal, the operation and the aftermath.
- Working length: about 52,000–60,000 words, governed by complete scenes. The operation is the book's
  climax and may run as long as it earns; rehearsal and aftermath have to be as developed as the fights
  were, not summaries. If a limit intervenes, save complete chapters and a precise continuation
  checkpoint, then resume the same run without beginning editorial review.
- Read first: Chapter 40 (supplied); `STATE_LEDGER.md` (Movement One to Five blocks, as reconciled to the
  repaired prose); `provenance/MOVEMENT-005-CONTINUITY.md` and `MOVEMENT-005-REPAIR.md` (the repair corrects
  the continuity file where they differ); `BOOK_MAP.md` (§3 "Complete ending" and §6 Movement Six govern
  intent); `CHARACTERS.md`; `UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md` (§7 and §9); `OWNER_BRIEF.md`. The manuscript wins over every
  record. Existing names are locked. Check manuscript, planning and provenance files for collisions before
  inventing a minor name. Nothing from the research corpus.

## Where we enter

It is the night of Sunday the 20th of the third month, 2681, at Saltmere. The National Measure has been read:
the Ropewalk School's first four won, Halden License second, and **Team Seven finished seventh of twenty-four
on 69, on the national license roll by one mark, warranted (as a roll four) for Wednesday**. The Charter
Board's finding named the dream (**the unroofed sky**, "for want of a better", no agreed meaning) and **warranted
a Barrel response at the Saltmere harbour pump house at the dawn slack on Wednesday the 23rd**, on the keeper's
leave: the Saltmere Company's officers; the Works' reader on the landing; the keeper's fitters on the cells; **the
Order's thirty-six jars of plain ash, given at a price said aloud**; any four on the roll may stand by name on the
Board's warrant and ash; no four is obliged. The sealed prediction (Hart and Mara and Peverell, 11th) opens at
noon on Wednesday: the dawn spring slack at 6.09 by the pump house clock, not before 6.07 nor after 6.12, lasting
18–22 minutes; the ring at the outfall (the Hoop) will rise from the mud and stand at four feet eleven; the water
inside will keep the bank's count, four and four, at fifty-seven to the minute or faster; nothing else in the
basin will keep a count. The Board has told nobody what the thing in the basin is, because nobody knows.

**The thing in the basin** stands up at each spring slack and grows each time (a hand high in the ninth month,
4' 1", 4' 6½"). It is keeping the harbour's time. It is the first Barrel-class expression on the page. The
harbour's thirty-six-cell bank (the Regiment, fifty-seven to the minute, in step since twenty past two on the
17th of the ninth month, 2680) has not dropped a stroke in six months and is the only thing standing between the
low town and the sea; the harbour water moves twice a day and is still only at slack, for about twenty minutes. The
Ferren method needs still water and a count that is not the caller's; Mara's twelve sheets failed here in the
winter because a harbour is not a storm drain. **Mara has decided not to read the bank herself: thirty-six hands,
each on its own count, taught by the 22nd, one jar of plain a cell.** Hart teaches what the quiet feels like; Mara
will stand on the landing with a watch and say *now*. Peverell's pumps will stop for eleven minutes at the slack by
her own hand; a foot of water in Ropery Row; "move the coal up a stair". The Harbour Committee has signed the Order's
price: **the thirty-six cells never to be stepped again while the pump house stands, each its own count, not two
alike.**

**Kiva Rowan** is seventeen, seated, unclassified, with a warm left hand (six mornings), a healing right, and
**the week's crossing kept for this Wednesday (the sixth was Thursday the 10th; one crossing in seven days at most;
nobody has asked for it yet)**. Aske's card: the seventh past the elbow, five nights' sulk. Three doors stay three
clean. Her dream is public: she put it on the table herself; she told the Board's commission about the white lights
(ferry only, lower, nearer, "I'll not have it said I saw more"); **the colour with no word is hers alone and not on
the table**; Mara has learned there is a white and a thing she was never told and will not ask. Two fevers near
Glory masters, the third on Sunday with no dream ("Nothing came"). Her doors lie down near Thrale and, near the
Proprietor, want to march and to beat. The disk is cold. She has made a yes/no log at a quarter to seven since Hart's
method (yes, every day). Her ten sheets of Pike's craft and the pass sheet are copied; Ivey, Yeo's catcher, has a
second; **Coombe's page** (the far edge belongs to the second, "the book is written for a sheet") is in every officer's
pocket.

**Team Seven** holds **5.15 drams** (sealed medical tin, 2.0 plain, unopened, bound *For a hand. Not for a bank, a
floor, a crossing or a sale*), and four people with offers: **Anwen** has said yes to Graywater (Doran Mace's ward line,
her own place, from the 1st of the fourth month; told Kiva first); **Jude** has the Saltmere Company's card
(Yeo; "come and find out"; will answer after Wednesday) and a frosted left arm; **Perrin** has taken down Halden's
offer and has the Municipal Works' (Rusk) up, with a cracked seventh rib and Aske's card against lifting; **Kellan**
(Halden License, second) refused Halden's junior-assessor card in writing and holds a white card with a Halden mark
whose contents are not on the page; **Pru** is "on a sheet". Dray ("On my feet.") and Carrow ("I want to be on the
wall") will be there. Rhea Sorn's card ends on the 30th; **she has not written Kiva's, and will not before the
30th**; Pike's hands close halfway; Orla's sponsor's line has been struck on evidence; Orla and Hart have each said one
true sentence and nothing is mended. **The Proprietor** has testified (he has dreamed it eleven times; read Annick in
2610; the 2611 settlement followed; "I'm sure of my reading. I'm not certain I'm right"); his left hand lies on a glass
case over the foundation cell and has not lifted for more than a minute since 2611; the foundation's claim on "all ash
exhibiting the Step, however produced" has not been relinquished. **Thrale**, who has been past her gate twice in her
life, is in Saltmere an hour at a time ("a day's work"); the Order's cellar holds four jars.

## What this movement is for

This is the end of Book Two. It has to deliver a complete, satisfying national-scale ending with its three meanings
paid (the competition, the nation counting what it has, and the beat itself), and it must not be a bridge to Book
Three. The movement has three parts of unequal weight that you may divide as the story wants: **rehearsal** (Monday and
Tuesday, in developed scenes), **the Slack** (Wednesday dawn, the longest and most sustained set piece in the book), and
**after** (the rest of the month and the last of the third month, to Thursday the 1st of the fourth).

### Rehearsal, with one bad assumption found in time and one that is not

Developed scenes of people learning to do a thing together that has never been done at this scale. Build the
operation from parts the reader already owns: the handed crossing (catch, carry, plant, three people with unlike doors,
one caller who has been inside both gaps; it costs almost no ash and a great deal of declared trust; it cannot be done by
strangers); Hart's quiet; Mara's thirty-six counts; Coombe's drills; Ivey's second; Yeo's closing crews; Pike's far
edge; Carrow's lay and Dray's feet; the Graving. **The Graving** (a dry dock roofed with tiers; a floating gate keeps
the harbour out; with the gate shut and the sluices closed it is a basin of still water whatever the tide does outside)
becomes the purse that catches the ash when its gate is shut. Find, in rehearsal, one bad assumption in time to fix it
and let one survive to the dawn. Nobody is rehearsed past what the cohort can bear.

### The Slack: the operation as the map gives it (intent, not choreography)

At the dawn low-water slack of Wednesday the 23rd, a mixed cohort from the Measure's roll and three professional crews
take Saltmere's harbour bank of thirty-six cells **out of step**. The water is still for about twenty minutes. The thing
standing in the basin has to come off the water first.

- **Students** stand the wall, carry, cut on a foreman's slate, and bag. **Kiva calls three handed crossings in a row and
  tests every mark first.** Students close no tear.
- **When a link fails, she supplies it herself: one certified crossing, on a sanctioned mast, read by the office's
  reader.** It is the first decisive thing she has ever done that was entirely inside her certificate. **When a second
  link fails, she makes a second crossing in the same hour, against her healer's card, with the price said first.**
  (A third failing link is not offered: three doors stay three clean.) Place the crossings by the calendar and by
  Aske's card, which Aske says twice.
- **Professionals** close the tear: fitters and keepers under Mara Rowan, Jessamy Hart and the harbour keeper
  Peverell fill thirty-six cells on thirty-six counts. Mara does not read the bank herself and does not touch a cell
  until she decides to; whatever she decides, she decides on the page.
- **The Proprietor holds the harbour's pumps on his own pattern** so that the low town does not flood. The operation
  cannot finish while he holds it, because his pattern is the Step. **He lets go: of the bank, and, in writing, of his
  foundation's claim on "all ash exhibiting the Step, however produced."** His limit is visible: his left hand on the
  glass case, not lifted for more than a minute in seventy years. What it costs him to lift it must be shown and must
  be real. **Master Thrale holds a ward across the dock mouth and cannot move while she does.** Both limits are on the
  page; no Glory master does a student's or a fitter's work.
- **For some seconds two Glory fields from unlike traditions touch. Every lamp on the coast shows a point of light.**
  Three people who have dreamed (Kiva, and the two masters) are seen to count together. Do not explain it.
- **The bank comes out.** The harbour's pumps stand (the Proprietor's hold ends); the low town takes water. **A
  professional died on this coast earlier in the book (Penhale); nobody dies at the Slack.** Carrow and Dray stand on the
  wall and each uses a piece of the other's method. Anwen leads a mixed ward line. Rhea takes a count from her own second.
  Jude, Perrin, Pru, Kellan and Noa have work in it that is theirs. The prediction opens at noon; read what is written
  against what happened, honestly, including where it was wrong.

### After

- **The Charter Board orders every municipal bank in the country put on its own count and kept there**: a slower,
  dearer, deliberately uneven grid. It is a real national answer and it costs light, money, and an industrial house
  its oldest property.
- **What it does not settle is whether the Proprietor was right.** He has believed for seventy-seven years that
  stepped lamps are the ones that go white instead of out. Do not settle it; do not make him a fool or a villain.
- **Kiva's cost:** the hand is gone to the wrist for three weeks and returns as before. What does not return is the
  rate: **Aske's card now reads one crossing in fourteen days.** Three doors remain three clean. The sequence was asked
  for twice in this book (the Proprietor in the third movement; a Glory master at the Slack) and refused twice.
- **The last of the third month.** Rhea Sorn and Kiva look again, as the card promised. Either may say no, and each says
  no to something. Rhea names no seat. She writes Kiva's name on the Charter Board's travelling commission, under another
  officer's hand, with *either of us* on it again. Kiva takes it on two conditions of her own (choose them; make them hers
  and not a pattern). **Team Seven's four go four ways by their own choices:** the offers on the table (Graywater for
  Anwen; the Company for Jude and what he asks about ash; the Works or elsewhere for Perrin; Kellan's white card; Pru's
  sheet) resolve on the page, each in a scene.
- **Relationships** to land: Kiva and Pike (each has been the other's second); Kiva and Rhea (a count taken, a name given
  away); Kiva and Orla (the sponsor's line struck; a certificate in Kiva's name); Kiva and Mara (watching her take the
  national risk without owning it; the seal broken by both, done); Kiva and Anwen (equals about to work in different
  places); Kiva and Perrin (envied, captain, a board won); Kiva and Kellan (he put his numbers where they hurt his house;
  a card); Kiva and Pru; Kiva and Dray (calls the count on her feet; not a friend); Kiva and Carrow (each uses a piece of
  the other's method); Kiva and Hart (what a ruined hand can be asked); Orla and Hart (no absolution).
- **The last image.** Wires vouch what the ship's engineer said (his unvouched line from Book One): ports over the water
  logged the same point of light in the same minute. A hull stands in from the south with a light on its bow that is like
  no lamp in Saltmere. Somebody else has dreamed, and has come to see who answered. **The disk is cold. Kiva writes that
  down.** The one on the hull is not met, named or explained.

## What must remain true / withheld

- One crossing in seven days at most (Aske's card), until the Slack's second crossing against it with the price said
  first; the card afterwards reads one in fourteen days. **No three-door. No fourth use.** The three clean stay three. If a
  Glory master asks for a fourth, she says no and says why. Nothing heals the two fingers, the lost Ropewalk ward, or any
  death. Costs are not reset.
- The **disk is cold** throughout, even beside two Glory fields touching: no origin, no nine rings, no first bearer's
  book. **The colour with no word stays hers alone.** "Breach" remains unused.
- Reserved (do not spend): the complete loss of the filter; pure Glory presence worldwide; the cause of the Fall; what the
  pressure is, why it keeps a count, who or what is attending; the disk's purpose; whether Hesper Annick died in 2461;
  any returning figure from the first trilogy; a doctrine that makes the schools obsolete; anything settled between
  nations; any international convergence beyond "it has begun" (the hull, the wire). Kiva's classification: no column is
  made for her.
- **Nobody is a villain:** not Halden, not the Works, not the Order, not the Board, not Dray, not Carrow, not the
  Proprietor, not the Committee. A Glory master on the page has a limit the reader can see. Every climax capability is
  shown and priced earlier (it is: the handed crossing, the week's crossing, the far edge, the quiet, the counts, the
  jars, the Graving, the lay, the hands).
- Students close no tear. Cuts are on a foreman's slate. Ledgers reconcile to the hundredth: the team's ash, the thirty-six
  jars, the Slack's yield and its shares, the cost of the crossings; check every sum, clock, weekday and count before it is
  spoken. The red bags stay evidence.
- Preserve injuries, cards, knowledge boundaries, dates and ash balances exactly. Dream knowledge moves only by a decision
  made on the page.

## What the author owns

Scene and chapter boundaries; how the eight chapters divide between rehearsal, the Slack and the after; the shape of the
thing in the basin ("not the Pit's square and not Stopgate's string abreast"; a Barrel-class expression that keeps the
harbour's time and comes off the water first); how the Graving purse works; what fails, and where; the bad assumption found
in rehearsal and the one that is not; the Proprietor's hand and Thrale's ward; the point of light; the aftermath's grid
order and its price; the commission and its two conditions; where each of the four goes; the last image's staging; and
humor, intimacy, and the small business of people who have been far from home for six weeks. Name and invent only what the
book needs (the ship's engineer and the one on the hull are roles, not yet names). Keep Kiva dominant but let the movement
carry cutaways where she cannot witness a decision: about **85–88% Kiva**, with **6,500–8,500 words** across the five
established lenses (Anwen, Kellan, Mara, Orla, Rhea). Natural cutaways: Mara on the landing with the watch; Anwen with the
mixed ward line; Kellan with the Proprietor and the claim; Orla with the Chair or Hart; Rhea writing Kiva's name. No new
lens. Anchor every non-Kiva section in its first sentence by name and a cue only that person has; leave the "Kiva:" chapter
title convention.

## Craft lessons from Movements Two to Five (measured, simulated, same-family)

- The sentence distribution has moved between bimodal and over-corrected (median 6–7 against the formula's 11; 40–46% at
  five words or fewer against 27.7%). The long climbs and passes read best aloud (35–55 words, one main clause); the
  planning, hearing and aftermath chapters trotted. Write the middle on purpose: clear 8–25 word narration sentences
  carrying one thought, a few well-built 35–55 word sentences in descriptive and reflective passages, short beats for
  contrast. Join runs of short reactive sentences when the thought is continuous. No *and … and* chains over about 45 words.
- Scenes: keep continuous action, hearings and dinners unbroken; break where place, time or viewpoint changes; developed
  scenes near 900–1,000 words before meaningful turns. Do not end the book in a survey of twelve short sections: let the
  last chapters be scenes.
- A shared idiom keeps spreading ("I'd want it said", "I'd sooner", "on the table", "said nothing", "before the water",
  "frost on a window", "expect nothing else"). Give each person a distinct voice: Jude diffident ("I'd want it said" only for
  his heaviest lines), Anwen "I want it said", Perrin alone "I'd like it known", Pru scarlet, Aske's price speeches, Orla
  rough and brief, Hart plain and gauge-like, Mara dry, Kellan sums, Coombe drills, Dray flat, Rhea one piece of advice a
  season. Ration "slowly", "said nothing", negation runs, and causal *since* (use *because*). Give each simile one home. Do
  not state the theme over action that has already shown it.
- Do not telegraph a turn twice and then restate it afterward; say each fact once where it matters. Hearings and evidence
  readings must not re-read what the reader has just seen. Each figure, rule and tally arrives once.
- Check the page against itself: a score that does not follow its events, a clock that does not reconcile, a knowledge
  source that cannot exist, a water that runs both ways. The counting readers test every sum first.
- Movement Five's slips, as lessons: a score that omitted a down (check every lane against the rule); a paper cited for what only
  four people heard in a shut shop (every knowledge source must exist on the page); a public list of occasions that left out the
  occasion the sealed page proves (a list a character speaks must agree with the record she points to); evidence read aloud three
  times across three scenes (say it once, where it matters); a declaration rule ("and no change") that made the later temptation
  unreal until a line made the cost explicit (state the rule and its price before the choice); a dropped reopening quotation mark
  where a tag was inserted mid-speech (scan quote parity). Make the operation's rules and prices plain before the choices that
  spend them.
- Door vocabulary: a **plank** is two like doors on one ground; a **stack** or a **lay** is unlike doors open and agreeing; a
  **ward** is the hand door's tray and people are not "wards"; "hand door" is the hand tradition, not Kiva's cold hand.
- The contests that worked had one idea a child could state and an adult could appreciate, geometry stated, both sides
  adapting, and a visible cost. Make the operation trackable in one reading: fix the bearings, say who is where, keep the
  clock.

## Formula and prose

The owner's exact O'Connor numerical formula governs this run. This is the end of the book: let the progression system
rise again where the operation turns on doors, ranks, crossings, licenses, ash and grading, without dumping terminology.
Keep the paragraph median near 18 words, developed scenes near 900–1,000 words before meaningful turns, and accessible
roughly seventh-grade readability. Measure after all eight chapters, never as a chapter gate.

## Output and stop

Write complete Chapters 41–48 to `manuscript/chapter-41.md` through `manuscript/chapter-48.md`. Then write
`provenance/MOVEMENT-006-CONTINUITY.md`, append an accurate runtime/session/prompt hash, word counts, viewpoint counts and
first-draft status to `AUTHORSHIP.md`, and append a Movement Six block to `STATE_LEDGER.md` from surviving prose only.
Record any plan departure, the exact ash ledger (the team's book, the jars, the Slack's yield and shares), the hand's state
and Aske's card after the Slack, the standings of every thread (each of the four, Rhea's commission, the grid order), every
injury and loss, and every new name. Do not review, score, repair, commit, push, publish, or begin Book Three inside the
author run.

### 1a. Aske's card at exit (written Thursday the 24th, 7 a.m., shown)

*The left hand, as Wednesday: gone to the wrist, not cold. Feeling begins an inch above. The arm aches to the shoulder; less
than last night. Three weeks, I think, from the 23rd. Then as before. From this day: one crossing in fourteen days, at the
most. For the year, at the least. The hand seen the next morning. The three doors are not on this card. They were never on
it. I said the price on Monday and on Wednesday, before. I said it right.*

## 2. Rehearsal and the Slack

**The one idea (Mara, Monday):** "It's a bucket with no bottom that holds water anyway, because the water's keeping the
town's time. If it spills in the harbour, the time goes back into the harbour, and the bank can't come out. If we cut it
where it stands, there's a Barrel of open ash in the water the pumps draw from, and that can cook a bank. So we pick it up
full, and we carry it out of the harbour without spilling it, and we empty it in the sink. The sink's the Graving."
**Brask's naming:** a cask with staves and a chime and no head; "the water's its head. And the head's keeping time."
**Mara's winter finding:** the six cells that went back in during the winter trial went back at the end of the slack,
because the ring lay down and gave back what it held.

**Bearings (on the page):** pump house at the basin's head; six pumps; thirty-six cells on two galleries; iron landing and
grating; the ring ten paces from the outfalls (a circle of Hart's stakes marks its rim). The Graving lies along the basin's
west side, its mouth at the south-west corner, **fifty paces** from the ring across the mud. Marks: **the catch** at the
ring; **M1**, a stake 25 paces west; **M2**, the stone sill of the dock mouth, 25 more; **M3**, Noa's chalk line **45 paces
inside**, beyond Thrale's forty (paced by the Order's sister). The last five paces before the sill are slurry where the
dock drains. Masts: the Circuit's travelling mast on the basin wall above M1 (Polglase); the Board's lane mast moved to the
dock floor against the south wall, ten paces from M3 (Noa); both sanctioned in writing by the Clerk.

**Rules and prices said before (Monday):** students close nothing; Ivey's crew closes the tear; students on the wall cut on
Yeo's slate; every plank names its far edge before its pass; Kiva calls, tests every mark first, and carries an Ember lit,
declared, as reserve to every carry, with a crossing to still if a plant fails; Thrale's reach forty paces; the Proprietor
holds the pumps from the quench to the salt and lets go on Mara's word; Rusk's correction: **each pump on its own six
cells** (Peverell's "eight" at the Salt Hall was the Ferren figure; on Monday she says she will say it was wrong in front
of the Board, in seven columns; that saying is not on the page).

**Bad assumption found in time (Tuesday dawn):** the catch was planned as Mallow's, "be the nearest thing". The held water
leans at the outfalls ("It's looking at the pipes," Dray). A ward lit on the landing (Anwen; Coombe's leave; Yeo's word;
a count of forty) does nothing until it keeps Dray's called count; then the tremble turns to it in four counts. Coombe:
*The plank keeps the thing's count, on a called count, or it's nothing to the thing. And it makes itself the thing the
thing wants. Said before.* Dray calls every pass from the wall.

**Bad assumption that survived:** that the Proprietor could take his pattern off the pumps and keep his hand on the glass.
Tuesday 3 p.m. test: one pump (the third), its six cells lit; fitters said *town* when he took it ("it's coming the other
way"), *mine* when he let go; **fifty-eight seconds**; Rusk: "I believe it. It's happened." Kellan: "What happens when you
let go of a pump that's only got you?" "Then it stops… That's simpler, not harder." At dawn: "It's one hold. Not two."

**The Slack (clock by the pump house; Noa's mast clock set to it at five):**

| Time | Event |
|---|---|
| 6.08 | Kiva's two fingers on M3 go quiet first ("Quiet," aloud); Noa: *eight minutes past*; Hart says it a breath after. Kiva tests M3, M2 (sill), M1 (stake): all still. |
| 6.09 | The ring up: **4 ft 11** (Hart's level; Polglase calls it). Grey staves, a frosted chime, the held water leaning at the outfalls. Kiva lights Ember (Polglase: *Read*). Ward line A lights; keeps Dray's count; the water turns to them on the fourth count. *Far edges.* "**Take.**" |
| 6.09–6.10 | **Pass 1.** Ward line A (Anwen, Edda, Hesketh, Sedge) slides under the foot; the cask slides a pace west onto them. "Nobody." Jude and Gower carry 25 paces (the grey wraps their knuckles from 15). "Nobody." Brask and Tolver plant at M1. *Planted.* Polglase: *ten past.* |
| 6.10–6.14 | The tear open where the ring stood: threads. Carrow (declared, read) and Dray cut on Dray's count as **one line, two people** (Polglase: "a lay. Between two… not an incident under any rule I've got"; Dray: "That's fairer"). **Ivey's crew closes the tear at 6.14**; Tamsin calls Ivey's far edge once. |
| 6.11 | **Pass 2.** Catchers' plank (Pru, Treen, Marten, Yarrow) at the four quarters; the Pascoes carry. At the sill the younger Pascoe goes knee-deep in slurry; the held water slops over the far lip; Pru: "Far edge." Wace steps back; **Bannock frosted hip to boot; his door goes out. Failure 1.** The Pascoes' wrap past the wrist. "Nobody." Kiva takes it on her Ember (knees to the slurry), shuts, gap, opens still; plants with Wace on the sill. Noa: *Cliff… a crossing, single, declared at six… eleven minutes past six and forty seconds. Read. The notch at five.* **Seventh crossing, Ember to still, inside her certificate.** Hand still warm. Aske at her side. |
| 6.12–6.13 | Ward line A runs the wall and comes down to the sill's dock side. **Pass 3** begins: ward line A takes from Kiva and Wace; Kiva shuts and is dark; Jude and Gower carry inward. |
| 6.13 | Lyle at M3: "*I can't. I'm saying it before.*" Her left foot back a yard. **Failure 2.** Jude and Gower keep walking. Kiva: "Count me ten." **Aske counts ten dark and says the price inside the count** (the hand; to the wrist; weeks, three; then as it was; one in fourteen; "I don't know what else"; "twice"). Kiva: "Heard." **Perrin declares from the wall** (*the second in the hour; against her card, with the price said first… I'd like it known it's mine as well*); Noa: *Read.* |
| 6.14 | Kiva lights, runs, catches them at 34 paces (their wrap past the wrist), "Nobody", takes it standing, carries 11 paces (the wrap to past her wrist), shuts, gap: **her left hand goes** (to the wrist, all at once, not cold); still opens; plants with Pettit on M3. Noa: *Cliff… the second inside the hour, declared by the captain's voice, the price said first. Fourteen minutes past six and fifty seconds. Read.* **Eighth crossing, Ember to still, against her card.** |
| 6.15 | Thrale comes down to the sill; her **ward across the mouth** (grey, wall to wall). Yeo's whistle, three short notes (Mara reads it at **6.15.40**). Kiva's and Pettit's still doors, at 45 paces, want to lie down; they hold. |
| 6.15–6.16 | Rhea: "Give it to me." Kiva reads the cask's count **through her still door** in the stone; "Now" in the rest; Rhea: "Cut." Jude and Gower cut north and south. Pike: "**Far edge**" (Kiva steps back; Pettit's boots frosted to the ankle). The held water and ash run down the dock floor and lie still against the ward. Noa: *sixteen minutes past.* |
| 6.15.40–6.19 | **Mara cutaway.** "Quench." The pumps miss one stroke and go on, on the Proprietor's pattern. "Draw" (old cans boxed as evidence). "Set." "Town or mine": *town*. 6.19.10: "Mr. Halden. Let go." |
| 6.19–6.22 | **Kellan cutaway.** Right hand off the flange at 6.19.11; the pumps go on: one hold. "Nobody salts." Mara: she will call it off at 6.24, on her name; she will not touch a cell. Adlam's fact: the hand comes up once a year for the cleaning of the glass, 51 seconds the longest. Kellan's sum ("Seventy years is twenty-five thousand days… This is three minutes"). |
| 6.22.11 | **The Proprietor lifts his hand.** A print of it fogged on the glass. **The first cell goes to its own count.** The pumps stop. His pulse stumbles (57, 61, 49, ragged). His count goes out across the basin to the ward. **Every lamp to a point for nine seconds** (the Graving's three rows of tier lamps, the Long Quay, Ropery Row's windows, the landing lamp). Noa: a hair on every pen, nobody on the mast. Three counting: Kellan sees the Proprietor's lips; Rhea sees Kiva's ("four, and a rest"); the Order's sister sees Thrale's and writes it. Disk cold. |
| 6.22.20–6.29 | **Mara cutaway.** "Salt." "Cards" (thirty-six counts, not two alike; Mara initialled each at midnight). "Town or mine": thirty-five *mine*; case nineteen, Loveday Hosken: "I can't tell" (Mara: "Say you can't tell. That's a word as well"), then "*Mine…* my mother's hymn." 6.25 thirty-six. **Peverell starts the pumps one at a time, each on its own six cells, 6.26–6.29.** Low town wet **6.22–6.29**. Hart: "like six bands at a fair." **Mara lays her reading hand on case nineteen after: "Mine."** Loveday's right hand starved white to the wrist. |
| 6.31 | Hart: *Ache.* The flood. |
| 6.35 | **Kellan cutaway.** The Proprietor writes, right-handed, on the pump house's fault paper: *The Halden Foundation relinquishes, from this day, its claim on all ash exhibiting the Step, however produced. It was never ours. We found it.* Witnessed, K. Renn. Pulse found **sixty-four** at 6.26. Left hand white, half curled, not put back. |
| 6.40–7.04 | The gate brought in on the flood by two Company boats; sunk onto its seating; Thrale steps back one pace for it; **seated at 7.04**; Thrale lets go after **forty-nine** minutes of ward, **fourteen past her hour** (in the town 74 minutes); carried to the Mole on the jar cart. "Write that you counted, and that you don't know what… That's all anybody should write." |

## 3. Who stood

On the warrant by name (Monday): **the Ropewalk first** (Carrow, Lyle, Tolver, Treen); **Halden License** (Pettit, Yarrow,
Hesketh; *Renn, by the Proprietor's leave, at the pump house; not on the mud*); **Old Four** (Brask, Wace, Edda, Gower);
**Holloway second** (Bolitho, captain; the two Pascoes, Embers; their ward and still door on the wall); **Team Seven**
(*Cade, P., captain: to declare for the four, and keep the brake. Not on the mud*; Pryce; Talley; Finch; *Rowan, K.,
catcher. Caller, if the officer will have me. One crossing kept, as certified*); **Twenty-Eight** (Marten, Sedge, Bannock,
Dray). **Not standing:** Westerley first (*We've a keeper at home who'd want us there*) and Holloway first (*With
respect*); the clerk wrote under each *Loses nothing by it.* Twenty-four on the wall.

Planks: ward line A (Anwen, Edda, Hesketh, Sedge); catchers' plank B (Pru, Treen, Marten, Yarrow); Embers A/C (Jude,
Gower), B (the Pascoes); still A (Brask, Tolver), B (Bannock, Wace), C (Pettit, Lyle); the wall (Carrow, Dray, Holloway's
two). Professionals: Yeo (officer; slate and whistle), Ivey's crew of five and Tamsin, Coombe, Rhea (officer of the purse),
Pike (Kiva's far edge), Aske (the sill), Mara (landing, watch), Hart (grating), Peverell (doorway, levers), thirty-six
fitters, Rusk. The Chair, the Clerk and the two Board members on the wall. Orla on her crate by Polglase's mast.

## 4. The sequence asked and refused

**At 5.40 on the Slack's morning the Proprietor asks Kiva** (from his chair at the Long Quay's end, thirty feet): "If two of
your links fail… and a third is failing… Will you do the three?" **"No."** Why: "if this morning needs my three, it's not a
method. It's me… The bank has to come out on things with handles… I don't know what a fourth costs… I'll not spend a thing I
can't price." He: "That was a girl saying no to a rich man. This is a catcher saying no to a method… I'll not ask again."
(The first refusal was the third movement's, on a Halden mast.) **Three clean, unchanged. No third-door attempt. No
fourth.**

## 5. The prediction, opened at noon Wednesday (the timber pond boards)

Named at the box by Mara: Hart, Peverell, Kiva, Loveday Hosken; by the Board: Yeo, Rusk, the Chair. The Clerk read the
paper in full, then each line against the record:

| Line | Found |
|---|---|
| Slack at 6.09, not before 6.07 nor after 6.12 | **6.08** (Kiva's hand at M3 first, then Hart's at the grating). Right in the limits, wrong in the minute. "I've never been early, and I was early. I don't know why." |
| 18–22 minutes | **23** (6.08 to the ache at 6.31), **not a clean figure:** Hart's hand ached 6.16–6.22 while the pumps went on the Proprietor's count; quiet again after, though six pumps ran on six counts ("I'll not give the Board a guess"). **Wrong.** |
| Ring at 4 ft 11, not less than 4 ft 10 | **4 ft 11.** Right. |
| It will lie down when the slack ends | **Not tested, by design** (Mara). |
| Water inside keeps the bank's count, 57 or faster | **57** (Dray's watch, every pass). Right. |
| Nothing else in the basin keeps any count | **Wrong, twice, by the operation's own doing:** the planks kept Dray's count from 6.09; the pumps kept the Proprietor's 6.16–6.22. |

The Clerk: "a better record than most sealed papers the Board has opened in thirty years." Then **the thing on no line:** Noa's
hair (6.22.11, nine seconds, every pen, nobody on the mast); every lamp to a point, seen by three thousand people (the Board
asks nobody the colour); and three separate written accounts (Rhea's slate; the Order's book in the Master's words; Kellan's
fault-paper sheet) that three people who have dreamed were seen to count together. **No line drawn.** All in the black box.

|---|---|---:|---|

Listen to her"); her fright at being looked at; **Lyle's left foot goes back an inch**; Lyle asks to be asked, before; Anwen tells Kiva (*M3: Lyle. Asked to be asked, before. Pryce has her far edge*). |
| Mara | 45 | 943 + 1,293 | The landing: quench, draw, set, "let go", the stall, her 6.24 line on her name; then salt, cards, thirty-six *mine*, the pumps, and her hand on case nineteen. |
| Kellan | 45 | 1,942 + 798 | By the chair: one hold; Adlam's 51 seconds; the sum; the lift; the first cell; the pulse; the lips counting. Then the relinquishment written and witnessed. |
| Orla | 46 | 1,004 | Wed 4 p.m.: **the Chair strikes the sponsor's line on evidence**; the second crossing entered on the next page (*not an incident*); then the gallery with Hart ("It doesn't make the Bench right." "No." "Good."). |
| Rhea | 48 | 844 | Wed 11 a.m., Graywater's tailboard: Coombe's commission sheet; Monday's slate line revealed (*The second called the far edge, once, before the bag. Not mine to keep*); she writes Kiva's name under Coombe's hand; Pike: "So would I. That's why it's Coombe's." |

**Viewpoint (by section, whitespace tokens, chapter headings excluded): Kiva 48,213 of 56,457 body words, 85.4%;
cutaways 8,244 (14.6%).** Inside the packet's 85–88% and 6,500–8,500. Every cutaway opens on its lens by name with a cue
only that person has. No new lens. The Tanyard section (ch. 47) is Kiva's.

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **Kiva Rowan** | Seventeen; seated; **unclassified**; **certificate in her own name** (Orla's line struck on evidence, Wed 23rd). Two crossings this movement (§1, §2); **left hand gone to the wrist from the 23rd for three weeks; the arm aches to the shoulder for days; then as before (the two fingers as they were).** Aske's card: **one in fourteen days**, for the year at least. Three clean; not done; asked once and refused. Right hand healed enough to write (badly). No fever after the Slack (Aske: *Two masters, and touching, and nothing*). **Disk cold throughout,** including at 6.22. Yes/no log: Mon latch *yes*; Tue (not stated); **Wed–Thu 1st *no answer*** (nine days of *no answer* by the 1st; she keeps it, on Hart's method). Reads a count through her still door (first time, at the cut). Fourth-column lines: Wed night *Cold. Both. As before.*; Thu 1st (the hull) *The disk is cold.* **On the Charter Board's travelling commission under D. Coombe from the 15th of the fourth month to the last of the ninth, on her two conditions (§10).** Goes home on the Works' transit (Friday the 2nd, the Board's fare). Her pass sheet's second edition corrects the first limit (a caller need not have been inside both gaps; somebody must keep the count who isn't carrying). |

| **Jude Talley** | Carried passes 1 and 3; knuckles white, cold not taken. **Yes to the Saltmere Company, Yeo's closing crew, from the 1st of the fourth month, six months**, with a line on the card: *to be told where every bag he carries goes after, and what it's for, by the officer, in writing, whatever the answer is.* Swept Mrs. Penhale's kitchen. ## 8. Ledgers

- **Team Seven's book:** held **5.15**, unchanged (rehearsal and Slack doors on the Board's ash, by the warrant). **Sealed
  medical (W, plain): opened Wed 7.25 for L. Hosken's starved right hand, by Aske; used 1.2; 0.8 left, resealed with
  Anwen's wax, bound as before.** Entry in Jude's hand, signed Cade and Rowan (right-handed). The book passed to Finch on the
  29th: *Held 5.15. Sealed 0.8, bound as before.*
- **Jars:** the Order's thirty-six combed at the gate Tuesday (third full, seventh silent), slept at the Mole hut, carried
  by thirty-six fitters by hand at 5.30, **thirty-six into thirty-six cells** at 6.22. Order's cellar: **4.**
- **Kiva's crossings:** seventh (6.11.40) and eighth (6.14.50), Ember to still each; on the Board's ash. Figures per
  crossing are not stated on the page (about 0.30 each by precedent).
- **The Slack's yield:** the purse bagged wet on catchers' own sheets (Pru, Treen, Marten, Yarrow, Wace) 7.30–11.00; combed
  at the gate 11.45: **286.4 drams; every dram sang on the seventh; red.** *No claim* (relinquished). **The Board's, as
  evidence. "The warrant pays no share on red… No share paid. No claim. Held."**
- **Old cans:** thirty-six drawn stepped, boxed, red-tagged, to the Board's press as evidence.
- **The Tanyard bag** (Mon 28th): one, Handful, combed red two days later.

## 9. The grid order (Friday 25th, the Salt Hall, the Clerk, eleven minutes)

Every municipal bank in the country (pump house, lamp office, drum, tenement box, tram shed) on its own count, no two alike
in any house, each pump on its own cells, **kept there**; no municipal bank stepped from this day nor joined by water or
iron to a stepped one; any fallen in to be taken out by the Board's officers as plain ash can be found; **every jar and tin
of plain declared to the Board within the year (declared, not taken), allotted to banks first and hands with them, and no
bank before a hand**; a **travelling commission under D. Coombe**, with readers and officers lent, to every municipal bank on
the roll within two years, writing in seven columns what was got wrong first.
**Price (Kellan):** about **a tenth of the light** (Ferren lost a ninth since the autumn; Saltmere the same since
Wednesday); **a third again in fitters' wages** (Peverell's book), the towns' to pay; **the foundation's municipal licences:
a hundred and twelve works stepped under licence since 2391**, its oldest income. ("Its oldest property was a cell under a
glass… It's not in the Board's order.") Counsel: the foundation will not contest; its own yards stay stepped. The
Proprietor's slip: *I am not certain I was wrong… On Wednesday they did neither.* The Clerk: "The Board doesn't say he was
wrong… a fitter's rule is all it's got." **Not settled whether he was right.**

## 10. The 30th and the commission

Rhea asked whether Kiva would want her to ask Graywater for her as Pike's second next winter: **Kiva: "No"** (she would stay;
Nairn can watch Pike's far edge). Kiva asked Rhea to come on the commission as an officer: **Rhea: "No"** ("Graywater's mine.
You're not."). The sheet (Rhea's hand, under Coombe's): *Rowan, K. Catcher. Caller of the pass in three… One door declared at
every response. Two crossings certified… one in fourteen days, by her healer's card; the three doors not to be asked. Dark
hands till her healer's card is up, from the 15th of the fourth month. Under D. Coombe's hand. Until the last of the ninth
month. Then we look again, and either of us may say no.* **Kiva's two conditions, said first, written by Coombe:** (1) at
every bank she teaches the call to two who are not her and leaves it in seven columns in that house's book, and the next
bank is called by somebody else where somebody else can; (2) the dream is hers and not the commission's; she will put a
thing on a table herself if there is a thing; nobody asks. *Accepted. D. Coombe, Hunt Marshal.* Signed K. Rowan and M.
Rowan (guardian). Rhea's name on it nowhere.

## 11. The last image (Thursday the 1st, about 6.55 p.m., the end of the Mole; Jude and Noa present)

The coast's lamp offices from Saltmere to Westerley, and ships in the roads, reported the same nine seconds (the previous
Thursday and Friday; in Noa's column). New wires from over the water via the cable office at Westerley: **three ports** (two
quoted; one in another tongue, translated) logged every lamp to a point at **6.22 Saltmere time on the 23rd, nine seconds**;
the third, the ship's engineer's port: *OUR HARBOUR BANK FELL INTO STEP 17TH NINTH MONTH LAST AT AN HOUR WHICH RECKONED TO
SALTMERE TIME IS TWENTY PAST TWO AFTERNOON. AS OUR ENGINEER REPORTED. VOUCHED.* Noa struck *not entered as fact*, neatly.
**A hull standing in from the south** on the evening flood, a light on its bow like no lamp in Saltmere (not white, not
yellow). Kiva's thought, unspoken: somebody aboard dreamed, saw the lamps go to a point, and came to see who answered. **Not
met, named or explained. The disk is cold. She writes it** in the fourth column, right-handed.

## 12. Knowledge boundaries, changed

| Fact | Now known to |
|---|---|
| The point of light; Noa's hair | Everyone (three thousand in the Graving; the Board; the coast; three ports over the water). |
| That Kiva, Thrale and the Proprietor were seen to count together | The Board (three written accounts, read at noon Wednesday), and everyone at the box. What they counted: nobody. |
| The relinquishment | The Clerk, the Board, counsel, Kellan, the Compact's court (nine pages). |
| The first cell on its own count | The Proprietor, Kellan, Adlam, counsel; Kiva (Saturday); the Salt Hall (Kellan's line). |
| Hart's hand does not ache near six counts | Hart, Kiva, the Board (at the box). |
| The engineer's line vouched | Noa, Jude, Kiva; the Board's wire office; Noa's column to every pump house and lamp office. |
| The colour with no word | **Kiva alone, still.** The commission is barred from asking about the dream. |
| Rhea's *Mine* | Rhea, Pike; Kiva (told by Rhea on the 30th). |

## 13. New names (checked against every manuscript, planning and provenance file in the repository and `universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md`; pending owner review)

- **Loveday Hosken** — fitter at the harbour pump house, case nineteen.
- **Tamsin** — Ivey's second, sixteen.
- **Bolitho** — captain of Holloway's second four (a tall freckled girl).
- **the Pascoes** — Holloway's second four's two Embers, brother and sister.
- Rejected during drafting for collisions: Vell (first trilogy), Merrin (Book One records), Agnes (Book One, Agnes Roake).
- Unnamed on purpose: the Order's sisters; the Company's cooper; the harbour masters over the water; the ship's engineer; the
  one on the hull; Holloway second's ward and still door.

## 14. Departures from the packet and items for attention

1. **Length:** 56,569 words (`wc -w`, headings included), inside 52,000–60,000. Rehearsal ch. 41–42 (14,173), the Slack ch.
   43–45 (21,367), after ch. 46–48 (21,029).
2. **Viewpoint:** Kiva 85.4% (at the packet's lower bound); cutaways 8,244.
3. **Sections:** 55 sections, 47 internal breaks (about 8.3 per 10,000 words; mean about 1,026 words). The Slack's passes
   are long continuous scenes kept unbroken.
4. **The Glory master who asks for the sequence at the Slack is the Proprietor again** (at 5.40, not during the passes).
   The packet allowed "a Glory master at the Slack". Recorded so the owner can confirm.
5. **New interaction properties (owner items):** Kiva reads a count through her still door (tied on the page to its
   established wanting-to-beat near the Proprietor); the Proprietor's pattern and his hand on the foundation cell are "one
   hold"; **the foundation cell goes to its own count when he lifts** (the "oldest property" made literal); Thrale's ward holds
   the dock's water and the rising flood as well as the count; Hart's hand does not ache near six counts; the Tanyard pack did
   not keep any count after the bank came out (implying local packs took the town's count); no fever after two masters
   touching.
6. **Universe-bible §6 revised in-world:** "Somebody who has been through both can call it" is found wrong on the page
   (Ivey calls with one door; the count does it). Kiva's sheet's second edition records it. For the owner to confirm before
   later books lean on the old line.
7. **Peverell's "eight cells" at the Salt Hall (ch. 36)** is caught and corrected in-world by Rusk (ch. 41). Ch. 36 was not
   edited.
8. **The prediction** is found right in three lines, wrong in two (length; "nothing else"), not tested in one. The length
   miss (23) is unexplained on the page.
9. **Formula measures** (sentence distribution, paragraph proxy, readability, progression and combat density,
   development cadence) were written toward and **have not been measured** in the author run. Only words, sections,
   viewpoint, quote parity and a few phrase counts were checked, for the records.
10. **Not done (reserved and kept):** no third door, no fourth; the disk cold; the colour unspoken; "Breach" unused; nothing
    about what the pressure is, what attends, the disk's purpose, Annick's death, the filter, or anything settled between
    nations; the hull not met; Kiva not classified.
